If Moyasar finds graphic design incompatible with selling books and food, live activation is refused after the build, policy and sandbox work, and the shop cannot open; selling food may need its own licence (owner must confirm with the authorities), which no gate names.
FIX: Rewrite E02's input to name the open eligibility question (the certificate's speciality against books and food, Moyasar's written confirmation) and add the food-licence question to E03 or a new gate; ask Anas to put both to Moyasar before the sandbox runs are scheduled.

### PLAN-GAPS-04 [medium] P10 plans browser Sentry in `src/instrumentation-client.ts` on every page, but the public pages have 1 to 3 KiB of a 150 KiB budget left and the SDK alone is about 25 to 35 KiB gzip
PLANS/WORK-PACKAGES.md:127 fix:small conf:high prior:AUDIT-1 S22.13 moved Sentry from server instrumentation to this client file; I49 (5) and I50 (2) record the headroom without linking it to P10
EVIDENCE: P10 Files: '`src/instrumentation-client.ts` (browser errors only)'; P10 Proof: 'public initial budget measured'; VERIFICATION.md:55: 'public initial hydrated JS <150KiB gzip ... No silent relaxation'. scripts/check-budgets.mjs:22 `PUBLIC_JS_GZIP_BUDGET_BYTES = 150 * 1024`, gating the largest page. Today checkout.html is 148.9 KiB (ISSUES I50 (2); docs/operations.md:1185-1186 '1.1 KiB of headroom') and the largest room page 147.4 KiB (I49 (5)). Sentry's own CI budget for `@sentry/browser` `init` is `limit: '35 KB'` gzip, 33 KB with tree-shaking flags (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/getsentry/sentry-javascript/develop/.size-limit.js, read 2026-10-07). Next runs `instrumentation-client` on every route before hydration, so it lands in every page's initial scripts.
IMPACT: As written P10 cannot pass both its file list and its budget proof: the client instrumentation fails `pnpm check:budgets` on every public page, and the likely improvisation under deadline is raising the budget, which VERIFICATION forbids without escalation.
FIX: Decide before P10: no browser Sentry (D23 itself names only checkout, webhook and scheduler errors, all in Edge Functions), or a reporter imported dynamically after a `window` error event (or only under /admin), outside the initial chunks and measured by check:budgets; record the choice in D23/P10.

### PLAN-GAPS-05 [medium] P10's hosted proofs need the hosted project the plan only sets up in P11, and no package owns a way to put the first owner and the first content on it
PLANS/WORK-PACKAGES.md:123 OWNER fix:small conf:high prior:I41 records the content half for P11; the ordering against P10, the owner bootstrap and the SMTP restriction are not recorded
EVIDENCE: P10: 'deploy a Pages preview and the functions with the owner's authorization, and measure function cold starts ... the pg_cron jobs, the Pages build time' and Proof 'The full CSP holds on the hosted preview'. The hosted setup is filed under P11: ISSUES I28, I32, I41 ('Without them the build stops on purpose (`getBookRoom`, `getHomeDoors` and `getContactPage` throw)'; src/lib/content.ts:199-209, 274-276). The only content loader refuses a hosted database (scripts/import-content.mjs:5 'Local only: refuses unless DATABASE_URL points at a loopback host'), and so does the only owner bootstrap (scripts/bootstrap-owner.mjs:24-27 'Refusing: supabase status API_URL is not a local host.'). Supabase's default SMTP 'will refuse to deliver messages to addresses that are not part of the project's team' (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/auth-smtp), so a hosted staff member gets no sign-in code before Resend SMTP on a verified anas.studio, whose DNS has no record yet. Neither P10's nor P11's file list names a hosted bootstrap.
IMPACT: A Pages preview built against the hosted project fails at build time until a published `rooms/book`, `home.doors` and `contactPage` exist there, and nobody can sign in to the hosted admin to publish them; P10 stalls or runs its 'hosted' checks against something else, and P11 meets the bootstrap gap on launch day.
FIX: Move the hosted setup (migrations, I28 Auth settings with Resend SMTP after the E01 DNS work, secrets, Vault, the first owner, the I41 content bootstrap) into an explicit step before P10's hosted checks (a HOSTED-0 step or its own package), and add a guarded hosted mode (an explicit `--allow-hosted <project-ref>` confirmation) to `import-content.mjs` and `bootstrap-owner.mjs`, or a written dashboard procedure.

### PLAN-GAPS-07 [medium] The package chain makes the launch wait for the Moyasar sandbox: P09's phase gate cannot pass without E02, and P10 and P11 depend on P09, contradicting D34
PLANS/WORK-PACKAGES.md:119 OWNER fix:trivial conf:high prior:none
EVIDENCE: P09 Proof: 'Phase 3 gate: all money/sandbox/fulfillment/service journeys pass; missing E02 cannot be waived.' P10: 'Depends P09.' (line 125); P11: 'Depends P10 and live E gates.' (line 137). D34 (DECISIONS.md:41): 'The site launches with the book page and store showing «قريباً»; E02 (Moyasar) blocks paid operation only, not launch.' P08's proof (line 107) also wants a 'Full book AND new non-book sandbox purchase reaches real confirmed mail/admin', which needs the hosted `payments` function and Resend on a verified domain; anas.studio has no TXT, MX or `_dmarc` record today (`nslookup -type=TXT anas.studio 1.1.1.1`, `-type=MX`, `-type=TXT _dmarc.anas.studio`, 2026-10-07).
IMPACT: Read literally, no package after P08 can be accepted until Anas's Moyasar account and a hosted sandbox exist, so the site-only launch D34 allows is blocked; read loosely, an orchestrator under pressure 'waives' E02 at P09, which the same sentence forbids. The next three packages start on an ambiguous gate.
FIX: State in WORK-PACKAGES which proofs move to an E02-closure checkpoint (the sandbox purchases, real mail, Apple Pay) and that P09 (free bookings and the paid path against the emulator), P10 and P11's site-only launch are accepted with E02 open and checkout off; keep 'E02 cannot be waived' for opening checkout.

### PLAN-GAPS-11 [medium] Physical orders take a free-text address with no National Address, which Saudi parcel carriers must refuse since 2026-01-01
src/components/store/CheckoutForm.tsx:713 OWNER fix:small conf:high prior:none
EVIDENCE: The only address input is `<label htmlFor="checkout-address">عنوان التوصيل</label>` with a 500-character textarea (CheckoutForm.tsx:713-727); `checkout_create` accepts any 5 to 500 characters without control characters (supabase/migrations/20261002110000_checkout_payment.sql:519-521) and snapshots it as `orders.address`. The Transport General Authority's decision, effective Thursday 2026-01-01: «إلزام كافة شركات نقل الطرود بعدم استلام أو نقل أي شحنة بريدية لا تتضمن العنوان الوطني» (Okaz, 2025-12-28, https://www.okaz.com.sa/local/na/2228688). D09, DATA-AND-SECURITY and E03 mention city rates and manual carriers only; no plan document mentions the National Address.
IMPACT: Every paid physical or signed order whose buyer typed a street description instead of his National Address cannot be handed to a carrier until Anas reaches the buyer; a parcel stuck past 15 days lets the buyer cancel under the E-Commerce Law's article 14 (owner must confirm), and a refund follows.
FIX: Add a required «العنوان الوطني المختصر» field (or the National Address parts the chosen carrier asks for) to the physical part of the checkout, validated in the quote, `checkout_create` and the order snapshot and shown on the order view; fix the exact fields with Anas's carrier (E03, Q38).

### PLAN-GAPS-12 [medium] No public page names the seller: the store shows no seller name, registration, address or contact means, which the E-Commerce Law asks an e-store to disclose
src/app/(public)/store/page.tsx:25 OWNER fix:small conf:medium prior:none
EVIDENCE: The seller's legal name, address and registration exist only in `finance.commerce_settings` (owner-only; src/components/admin/CommerceSettingsForm.tsx:183-215) and in order snapshots; a grep for seller fields in src/components/store, src/components/site and src/app/(public) finds none, and the store page renders only the title band and product cards (store/page.tsx:25-71). The receipt adds only «البائع» and «رقم التسجيل» (supabase/functions/_shared/email.ts:377-383), not the address or a delivery date. E-Commerce Law (https://laws.boe.gov.sa/BoeLaws/Laws/LawDetails/360de590-0286-4fa5-a243-aa9100c31979/1, read 2026-10-07): article 6, the provider discloses in its e-store its name or distinguishing mark, its address (unless registered with an authentication body), its means of contact and its register details; article 8, an invoice after the contract with each item's cost, the total with fees and delivery, and the delivery date and place. No plan document mentions either article.
IMPACT: When checkout opens, the store sells under a name and address no buyer can see before paying, and Moyasar's pre-live review looks at 'communication channels' (FAQ). Whether a published seller block or the Saudi Business Center's free e-commerce authentication certificate (which would spare publishing a home address) is required for a freelance-document seller is for the owner to confirm; this is not legal advice.
FIX: Before checkout opens, decide with Anas (and an adviser if needed) what the store discloses; add a seller block (name, registration, address or authentication number, contact means, complaints channel) to the store and policy pages, fed by a public CMS field or a published copy of the seller settings; add the delivery address and expected date to the receipt of physical orders.

### SUPPLY-01 [medium] The second WhatsApp export (353 MB zip) and its sorted media are kept out of Git only by the local .git/info/exclude, not .gitignore
.gitignore:42 fix:trivial conf:high prior:AUDIT-1 G4.1 (its fix moved only the first export's rule into .gitignore)
EVIDENCE: .gitignore:41-42 ignores only `/BOOK_ASSETS/ANAS_WHATSAPP_CHAT/`. The working tree holds `BOOK_ASSETS/WhatsApp Chat with انس القرني - LATEST .zip` (353,392,497 bytes, 2026-10-02) and `BOOK_ASSETS/SORTED_2026-10-02/` (48 files). `git check-ignore -v` names `.git/info/exclude:18 BOOK_ASSETS/WhatsApp Chat*.zip`, under the comment `# 2026-10-02: the second WhatsApp export (holds private data, D42) and what was sorted from it; local only.` SORTED_2026-09-21, v2-design, v3-art, v4-art and V4_WITH_MAIN_DESIGN are likewise excluded only there (lines 7-15).
IMPACT: .git/info/exclude is per clone and never versioned. In any fresh clone (another machine, a re-clone after a history rewrite) with BOOK_ASSETS copied back, one `git add -A` or `git add BOOK_ASSETS` commits a WhatsApp export with third-party contact cards and voice notes and pushes it to GitHub: the exposure D42 had to purge with a whole-history rewrite and force-push.
FIX: Move the private-material rules into .gitignore beside line 42: `/BOOK_ASSETS/WhatsApp Chat*.zip`, `/BOOK_ASSETS/SORTED_2026-10-02/`, `/BOOK_ASSETS/SORTED_2026-09-21/` and the other local-only folders, as AUDIT-1 G4.1 suggested.

### SUPPLY-05 [medium] restore-check rehearses a hosted backup on the CLI's default Auth, Storage and Postgres images, not the linked project's versions, so version skew fails a good backup
scripts/restore-check.mjs:419 RUNTIME fix:small conf:medium
EVIDENCE: Line 419 `supabase init --workdir <tmp>`, line 423 `supabase start --workdir <tmp>`: the scratch project has no supabase/.temp. CLI v2.106.0 pkg/config/config.go:658-675 swaps the Postgres, PostgREST, GoTrue and Storage images (and Storage's target migration) for the linked project's versions only when supabase/.temp/{postgres,rest,gotrue,storage}-version exist; internal/link/link.go:119-167,285 writes them on `supabase link`. The schema dump excludes auth and storage (pkg/migration/dump.go:169 uses InternalSchemas), so those tables come from the scratch images while data.sql's `COPY "auth"."..." (columns)` lists come from the hosted project, and psql runs with ON_ERROR_STOP=1 in one transaction (restore-check.mjs:147-149).
IMPACT: Once the hosted project runs a newer GoTrue or Storage than the images bundled with the owner's CLI (hosted services upgrade continuously; the docs pin the CLI at 2.106.0), a COPY names a column or table the scratch schema lacks, psql stops, and `pnpm restore-check` reports `The psql restore failed` for a backup that would restore into a new hosted project. The owner cannot prove any backup for the P06/P11 restore and RTO gates, or learns to ignore the tool.
FIX: After `supabase init` (line 419), when manifest.source is `linked`, copy the repository's supabase/.temp/{postgres,rest,gotrue,storage}-version and storage-migration into <scratch>/supabase/.temp/ before `supabase start`, and print the versions used.
PROOF: In a disposable checkout write a GoTrue tag newer than the CLI default into supabase/.temp/gotrue-version, `supabase stop && supabase start` (the dev stack now runs that GoTrue and its auth schema), `pnpm backup --local`, then `pnpm restore-check <file>`. Wrong result: `The psql restore failed` with `column ... of relation ... does not exist` or `relation "auth.<table>" does not exist`.

### SUPPLY-07 [medium] The real-restore runbook never deploys the Edge Functions, re-points and rebuilds the Pages site, moves the Resend webhook or applies migrations newer than the backup
docs/operations.md:1332 fix:small conf:high
EVIDENCE: Steps 1-9 (lines 1334-1413) cover extract, psql restore, Vault, pg_cron, `supabase secrets set` (with the Moyasar webhook address), Auth settings, object upload, the deletion ledger and cleanup; none runs `supabase functions deploy`, although Supabase's guide lists Edge Functions as migrated separately (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/migrating-within-supabase/backup-restore). The site calls `${process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL}/functions/v1/contact` (ContactForm.tsx:107; quote.ts:318,399-400 with NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY), inlined at build. The Resend endpoint is `https://<project-ref>.supabase.co/functions/v1/resend-webhook` (operations.md:338-342); step 5 only re-sets its secret. Nothing says to link the new project and `supabase db push` migrations committed after the backup.
IMPACT: An owner restoring after losing the project ends, following the runbook line by line, with a database but no functions (contact, checkout, payments, outbox, orders all 404), a public site still calling the dead project, bounce and complaint events posted to the old address (suppression stops updating), and a schema older than the deployed code. Step 5's smoke test fails with no step explaining why, inside the RTO<=4h of VERIFICATION.md:59.
FIX: Add runbook steps: `supabase link --project-ref <new>` and `supabase db push` for migrations newer than the backup; `supabase functions deploy` for every function; set NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL and NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY in the Pages project and rebuild; move the Resend webhook endpoint to the new ref and store its signing secret; then the smoke test.

### SUPPLY-10 [medium] The pinned package manager, pnpm 10.33.0, carries 18 published advisories (10 high), all fixed in 10.34.5; it was already vulnerable when pinned
package.json:6 fix:trivial conf:high
EVIDENCE: package.json:6 `"packageManager": "pnpm@10.33.0"` (set in 2cfed29, 2026-09-22; repeated in docs/development.md:12); CI installs it via pnpm/action-setup (ci.yml:33-36) and the owner's global pnpm is 10.33.0. OSV (api.osv.dev, npm pnpm 10.33.0) lists 18 advisories published 2026-06-26 to 09-02, e.g. GHSA-vq4v-j7r6-jq4m (high, fixed 10.34.5: a tarball dependency's manifest name escapes node_modules and overwrites files such as .git/hooks/pre-commit, `even with --ignore-scripts`), GHSA-5wx6-mg75-v57r (high, fixed 10.34.2: allowBuilds approval reused by a colliding locator), GHSA-c59q-g84q-2gj5 (high, lockfile depPath traversal), GHSA-rxhj-4m44-96r4 (high, malicious patch file). `npm view pnpm dist-tags`: latest-10 is 10.34.6.
IMPACT: Not reachable today: all 577 lockfile entries resolve from the registry with integrity and the one patch is ours. It becomes reachable at the next dependency update or agent-written lockfile/patch change: a compromised package pulling a crafted tarball, or a crafted lockfile entry, writes files outside node_modules during `pnpm install` on the owner's machine (where .env holds the Cloudflare token and Moyasar keys) or in CI, past the onlyBuiltDependencies policy the project relies on.
FIX: Set `"packageManager": "pnpm@10.34.6"` (ideally with its `+sha512.<hash>` suffix), update docs/development.md:12, and confirm `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` leaves the lockfile unchanged.

### TEST-DB-01 [medium] The non-negotiable 'no runtime DDL' invariant has no test, and table deny-by-default has no catalog-wide test (only functions do)
tests/integration/audit-fixes.test.ts:107 fix:small conf:high
EVIDENCE: PLANS/VERIFICATION.md:22 requires 'Direct SQL tests separately prove deny-by-default public access, `finance` isolation, server-only functions executable by `service_role` alone, and no runtime DDL.' A grep of tests/integration, tests/unit and tests/e2e for has_schema_privilege, pg_class, relrowsecurity, pg_default_acl, 'ddl' or any CREATE check finds nothing. The only catalog-wide check is S01.5 (audit-fixes.test.ts:107-142), which enumerates pg_proc for PUBLIC and anon EXECUTE only; tables are checked one by one per file (checkout.test.ts:810-833, payment.test.ts:484, forms.test.ts:197).
IMPACT: A future migration that grants a table to anon/authenticated without RLS, grants CREATE on public or finance to an API role, or adds dynamic DDL in a security-definer function passes the whole suite; the acceptance gate claims a proof that does not exist. No current breach was found (no `execute format` in the migrations; 20260924130000:7-12 revokes table defaults for anon/authenticated).
FIX: Add one catalog test beside S01.5: (a) has_schema_privilege(r, s, 'CREATE') is false for r in anon, authenticated, service_role and s in public, finance; (b) every public/finance table on which anon or authenticated holds a privilege has relrowsecurity = true, and the set of (role, table, privilege) equals a committed allow-list; (c) has_schema_privilege(anon|authenticated, 'finance', 'USAGE') is false.
PROOF: To see whether the property itself holds today: select r, has_schema_privilege(r,'public','CREATE') pub, has_schema_privilege(r,'finance','CREATE') fin, has_schema_privilege(r,'finance','USAGE') fin_usage from unnest(array['anon','authenticated','service_role']) r; any true in pub/fin breaks 'no runtime DDL', a true fin_usage for anon/authenticated breaks finance isolation.

### TEST-DB-02 [medium] HTTP integration tests run against whatever code the edge runtime last loaded; nothing detects a stale runtime
tests/integration/support.ts:26 RUNTIME fix:small conf:medium
EVIDENCE: PLANS/HANDOFF.md:28 and docs/development.md:329-332: 'The local edge runtime does not reload `_shared` on its own: after an edit under supabase/functions, docker restart supabase_edge_runtime_ANASAQ.ME before any test that calls a function over HTTP'. payment-http, checkout-http, refunds-http, orders-http, disputes-http, notify-http and staff-admin only read status.FUNCTIONS_URL (support.ts:26-36) and fetch it; no test compares the running functions with the working tree (no version probe, no container start time vs file mtime).
IMPACT: A change under supabase/functions/_shared that breaks the webhook, checkout, refund or download path passes `pnpm test:db` when the restart is forgotten: unit tests import the new code, the HTTP tests prove the old, and the P08 money invariants get false acceptance evidence. A writer is editing _shared/payments.ts in the working tree during this audit, which is exactly that situation.
FIX: In support.ts, the first time FUNCTIONS_URL is used, compare `docker inspect -f '{{.State.StartedAt}}' supabase_edge_runtime_<project_id>` with the newest mtime under supabase/functions and throw 'restart the edge runtime' when a file is newer; or make the test:db script restart the container first.
PROOF: Stack up: note the container StartedAt; edit a reply string in supabase/functions/_shared/payments.ts (scratch worktree); run payment-http.test.ts without restarting: it passes and the function still answers the old string (expected wrong result: no failure, no warning).

### TEST-DB-11 [medium] The coupon-limit race test cannot fail without the coupon lock: both carts hold the same variant, whose row lock already serializes the two creates
tests/integration/checkout.test.ts:1125 fix:small conf:high
EVIDENCE: checkout.test.ts:1123-1137 races two creates whose carts are both `[{ variantId: variant, quantity: 1 }]` with one coupon of usage_limit 1. checkout_create locks the cart's variants first and the coupon second (20261002110000_checkout_payment.sql:507 `perform 1 from public.product_variants v where v.id = any(v_ids) order by v.id for update;`, :509 `perform 1 from public.coupons c where c.code = v_code for update;`) and only then counts uses (:512 checkout_price -> finance.coupon_uses). The second create waits on the shared variant row until the first commits and sees its held redemption whether or not line 509 exists. The loser may also answer QUOTE_CHANGED, which a purely sequential run produces.
IMPACT: VERIFICATION.md:25 lists 'true simultaneous last-stock/coupon requests' as non-negotiable, and the coupon row lock is the only guard of a usage limit (coupon_redemptions has no count constraint, unlike refunds' refunds_in_flight_* indexes). Dropping or reordering that lock lets two buyers of different products redeem a limited coupon past its limit with the suite green; payment then commits both by design (payment.test.ts:1612).
FIX: Race two carts with different variants and the same limited coupon, forced: h.holdLock('select 1 from public.coupons where id = $1 for update'), start both creates, assert both 'blocked' via settledWithin, release, expect exactly one ok and the other COUPON_EXHAUSTED.
PROOF: On a scratch local database re-create public.checkout_create without line 509; run `vitest run --mode db tests/integration/checkout.test.ts -t 'coupon limited to one use'`: it still passes (expected wrong result). The two-variant forced version fails without line 509 and passes with it.

### TEST-E2E-06 [medium] No test can see content hidden by opacity or clip-path, so the non-negotiable "no home section hidden behind JS" is unproven
tests/e2e/public.spec.ts:163 fix:small conf:high prior:none
EVIDENCE: VERIFICATION.md:23 lists "no home section hidden behind JS" among the non-negotiable invariants. For / with JavaScript off the suite has only public.spec.ts:161-163 (h1 toHaveCount(1) and `expect(await page.locator('main p').count()).toBeGreaterThan(3)`, a DOM count that also counts display:none) and visual.spec.ts:67 (`await expect(page.locator('h1')).toBeVisible()`); no-js.spec.ts never opens /. Playwright counts opacity:0 as visible (playwright.dev/docs/actionability: "Elements with opacity:0 are considered visible"). Measured in Chromium 153 (scratch measure/visible-hidden.cjs): isVisible() is true for opacity:0, for clip-path: inset(100% 0 0 0) and for opacity:0 + translateY(32px), the start states src/components/weave/motion.ts FROM uses.
IMPACT: A change that leaves a home section, or any reveal, at its hidden start state until a script runs (or forever) passes public.spec, visual.spec and no-js.spec; visitors without JavaScript, and everyone when a reveal never plays, would see blank bands while the acceptance battery is green. Today motion.css keeps content visible without JS, so this is a missing proof, not a live defect.
FIX: In the JS-off tests (and one with motion on), wait for document.getAnimations() to finish, then assert for every main section, [data-enter] and [data-reveal] that computed opacity is 1, clip-path is none or inset(0px), visibility is visible and the box is non-empty; run it on / and every room.

### TEST-E2E-11 [medium] CI proves no database, money, security or browser behaviour, and its comment says that proof also runs on the hosted deployment, which no test can do
.github/workflows/ci.yml:111 RUNTIME fix:small conf:high prior:AUDIT-2 GAP-G1-11 (status refuted, run2 refuted/low, when only staff-admin needed the edge runtime); P08 has since added 7 HTTP files
EVIDENCE: ci.yml runs install, lint, typecheck (tsconfig includes tests/**, so the specs only compile), vitest unit, check:frozen, check:copy, deno check of every function, `supabase start -x realtime,imgproxy,studio,edge-runtime,logflare,vector,supavisor,postgres-meta,mailpit` (every migration applies to a fresh Postgres), db:import, db:env, build, check:export, check:budgets. Lines 111-114: "Not run here, and deliberately so: `pnpm test:db` and `pnpm test:e2e` ... that proof runs locally against the disposable Docker stack ... and on the hosted deployment." test:db cannot run against a hosted database (vitest.config.ts:16 assertLocalTestDatabase(): TEST_ENV must be "local", DATABASE_URL loopback) and every e2e spec importing helpers.ts refuses a non-local `supabase status` (helpers.ts:27-30). Of the 30 integration files only 8 call a served function over HTTP (staff-admin via support.ts:156, checkout-http, disputes-http, notify-http, orders-http, payment-http, refunds-http, order-emails:1130); the other 22 need only the Postgres, Data API, Auth and Storage this job already starts.
IMPACT: Every push is green whether or not the RLS policies, grants, checkout/payment/refund SQL and the invariants of VERIFICATION.md:20-31 still hold; a migration that drops a policy or breaks a money function merges with a green check and is found only at the next manual battery (P09-P12 will add migrations). The comment tells a reader that a hosted run covers what CI skips; no such run exists or can exist.
FIX: After check:budgets add `TEST_ENV=local DATABASE_URL=postgresql://postgres:postgres@127.0.0.1:54322/postgres pnpm exec vitest run --mode db --exclude "tests/integration/{staff-admin,checkout-http,disputes-http,notify-http,orders-http,payment-http,refunds-http,order-emails}.test.ts"` (no new service; vitest 5.0.1 has --exclude), and correct the comment to say what runs where. The full suite later (plan gap).
PROOF: On a fresh ubuntu-24.04 runner (or locally after `supabase start -x <the same list>`, db:import, db:env), run the vitest command above: expect the 22 files to pass with no edge runtime. Then add a migration `drop policy ... on public.staff` and push: today CI stays green; with the step it fails.

### TEST-UNIT-05 [medium] staff-admin's owner and TOTP gate has no CI test: the unit test mocks it to 'owner, fresh TOTP', and the integration test covers only an editor and an aal1 owner
tests/unit/staff-admin-function.test.ts:14 fix:small conf:high prior:AUDIT-1 X5.15 added operations and revoked callers to staff.test.ts (the Data API), not to the staff-admin function.
EVIDENCE: `vi.mock('../../supabase/functions/_shared/staff.ts', () => ({ staffFromRequest: async () => ({ userId: ..., role: 'owner', recentTotp: true }) }))` replaces the caller's identity in every case. As a result, staff-admin/index.ts:51 (`if (staff.role !== 'owner') return fail(403, ...)`) and :52 (`if (!staff.recentTotp) return fail(403, 'STEP_UP_REQUIRED', ...)`) never run for a non-owner in the unit suite. The only other coverage, tests/integration/staff-admin.test.ts:17 and :25, calls the function as an editor and as an aal1 owner. No test calls staff-admin as operations, as a revoked member (role null), or as an aal2 owner whose TOTP is older than five minutes, and CI does not run test:db (.github/workflows/ci.yml, closing comment).
IMPACT: These two lines are the only guard on inviting staff, changing roles and revoking members: the SQL cannot see aal/amr, as admin.ts's header also says of its own gates. A one-line regression such as writing the check as a denylist (`if (staff.role === 'editor')`) would let an operations member call set_role on themselves and become owner. Dropping line 52 would let a stolen aal1 owner session invite an attacker. Lint, typecheck, the unit suite and CI would all stay green, and the local integration file would still pass with the denylist. PLANS/VERIFICATION.md lists as non-negotiable: 'Real JWTs for anon/editor/operations/owner/revoked through ... every Edge Function; forbid self-promotion ... and owner-aal2 bypass'.
FIX: Make the identity a per-test value in the unit mock (hoisted, like `hoisted.client`) and add cases: editor, operations and null role -> 403 FORBIDDEN; owner with recentTotp false -> 403 STEP_UP_REQUIRED; null identity -> 401. Each case should assert that no client call (createUser or update) was made. Add operations and revoked callers to tests/integration/staff-admin.test.ts.

### TEST-UNIT-06 [medium] No test isolates staffFromRequest's `active` check: the only fake staff row is always an active owner
tests/unit/service-client-errors.test.ts:28 fix:small conf:high prior:lead:TEST-UNIT-06 (rated low there; raised because this line is the only guard for staff-admin, status, stats and paid-file-ticket)
EVIDENCE: authReturns() always answers the staff lookup with `{ data: { role: 'owner', active: true }, error: null }`. No unit test feeds `active: false`, an unknown role, a staff lookup error (STAFF_LOOKUP_FAILED) or a header without 'Bearer '. The check at staff.ts:47, `const role = staff.data?.active && ROLES.has(staff.data.role) ? ... : null`, is therefore unpinned. The one integration case with a revoked owner and a live session (tests/integration/disputes-http.test.ts:196-201) goes through dispute-record, whose SQL rechecks the actor, so it answers 403 even without the function's own check. staff-admin.test.ts checks a revoked member only through the Data API (`current_staff_role`) and a new sign-in.
IMPACT: For staff-admin (invite, set_role, set_active: direct service-role table writes), the owner-only `status` and `stats`, and `paid-file-ticket` (a signed upload URL with no SQL call), this line is the only thing that stops a member who was revoked a moment ago but whose access token is still valid (until it expires). Dropping `staff.data?.active &&` in a refactor passes every unit and integration test, and a revoked owner with a fresh TOTP could then restore themselves with set_active. D13 says a revoked member loses access on the next call.
FIX: Add unit cases to service-client-errors.test.ts: `{role:'owner', active:false}` -> role null; `{role:'superuser', active:true}` -> role null; no staff row -> role null; a lookup error -> throws STAFF_LOOKUP_FAILED; 'Basic x' or no header -> null without calling getClaims. Optionally add one integration case: revoke an owner who has a live aal2 session, then call admin `status` and staff-admin and expect 403.

### VENDOR-PAY-01 [medium] SADAD is on by default in Moyasar test mode and appears on the hosted invoice page; its payments cannot be refunded or voided through the API, and nothing in the plan, runbook or code knows it
docs/payments-runbook.md:298 OWNER RUNTIME fix:small conf:high prior:none (wave A EF-MONEY-12 covers a failed invoice, not SADAD/on_hold)
EVIDENCE: docs.moyasar.com/docs/guides/sadad-bill/basic-integration.md (fetched 2026-10-07), line 158: 'If you use Moyasar invoices, SADAD appears on the hosted invoice page automatically when enabled. The bill's expiry matches the invoice (capped at 29 days); the invoice goes `on_hold` while awaiting payment, then `paid`.' Line 184: 'Refund, capture, and void — SADAD bills are paid out of band; calling them returns an `unsupported_operation` error.' overview.md:75: 'On by default in test; contact support to enable live.' The bill's expired_at is a date (last day to pay). Repo: runbook step 3 'Live' (payments-runbook.md:298) and 'Before live' (312-317) make no payment-method decision; contract open item 6 still asks what on_hold means (P08-CONTRACT.md:110); refunds.ts:180-182 turns any 4xx into REFUND_REFUSED; refund_record_external answers NO_DELTA unless the provider's refunded total rose; the emulator never produces on_hold or a sadadbill payment.
IMPACT: Sandbox runs (test mode, SADAD on by default): a tester who picks SADAD leaves the invoice on_hold, and a refund of that order answers «لم يتم». If SADAD is ever enabled live: a SADAD buyer can never be refunded from the admin; a bill paid later the same day (after the 20-minute hold released the stock) lands as a late payment, often paid_needs_resolution, whose only resolution (a refund) is impossible through Moyasar; an out-of-band bank refund cannot be recorded (NO_DELTA), so the order stays paid, its file stays granted and gross keeps the money.
FIX: Record in contract section 2: on_hold = SADAD bill awaiting payment; payment status expired = SADAD bill unpaid; SADAD refund/void unsupported. In the runbook: keep SADAD disabled on the live account unless Anas accepts manual out-of-band refunds (owner decision); add a sandbox run that picks SADAD (observe on_hold, the ~5-minute payment_paid) and keep the refund run on card payments. If SADAD is kept, add an owner action that records a refund made outside Moyasar (a succeeded refund with source 'manual') before launch.
PROOF: Supervised sandbox run with the test key: create an invoice of at least 10000 halalas through the client, open its url, choose SADAD. Expect GET /v1/invoices/<id> to read on_hold with an initiated sadadbill payment, then payment_paid about 5 minutes later. Then POST /v1/payments/<that payment id>/refund {"amount":100}: the result that breaks the plan's refund path is 400 unsupported_operation.

### VENDOR-PAY-02 [medium] Visitor-triggered confirmation mail to unverified addresses shares the Resend account with receipts and sign-in codes, and Resend pauses an account whose bounce rate passes 4% or spam rate 0.08%; no brake exists and the residual counts only the quota
supabase/migrations/20261002150000_notifications.sql:114 fix:small conf:medium prior:Related wave A abuse paths DB-OPS-09 (Gmail aliases) and CLIENT-SEC-01 (sign-in codes drain the quota); neither covers Resend's bounce/spam-rate pause
EVIDENCE: notify_confirm_queue's only brakes are per address (1/day, 5/30 days) and 30 a day in all: `if not finance.rate_limit_take('notify-confirm:all', repeat('0', 64), 30, interval '1 day')` (notifications.sql:114); notify_subscribe accepts any syntactically valid address for an out-of-stock variant at 5 an hour per IP (:194). resend.com/docs/knowledge-base/account-quotas-and-limits.md (fetched 2026-10-07): 'All accounts must maintain a bounce rate of under 4% ... Maintaining a bounce rate above 4% may result in a temporary pause in sending' and 'spam rate of under 0.08% ... may result in a temporary pause in sending'. docs/operations.md:1189-1194 treats the abuse as quota only ('The caps protect the 100 a day'). Supabase Auth sign-in codes use the same account over SMTP (I28).
IMPACT: While any variant is sold out, someone passing Turnstile 30 times a day can sign up 30 non-existent mailboxes (random@gmail.com); each confirmation hard-bounces. At a small store's volume (tens of legitimate sends a week) the bounce rate is far above 4% within a day or two, and a single spam complaint from a person signed up by someone else exceeds 0.08% of a month's mail. A pause stops every receipt, order link, shipping and refund notice, owner alert and staff sign-in code until Resend lifts it.
FIX: Brake the visitor-triggered tier: in notify_confirm_queue (and outbox_claim for priority 2) skip notify_confirm while the last 7 days' notify_confirm rows with delivery bounced or complained exceed a small number (e.g. 3), queue one owner alert when it engages, and add the bounce/spam-rate rule to operations.md's residuals and the owner home's mail line. Optionally lower the 30/day cap.

### VENDOR-PAY-07 [medium] The dispute procedure only records chargebacks; Moyasar's platform terms give the merchant three business days from the information request to answer with documents, else the claim is lost with a 200 SAR fee, and 30 days to object to a statement
docs/operations.md:991 fix:small conf:high prior:none; wave A DB-OPS-12 notes the buyer export lacks download history
EVIDENCE: operations.md:991-1006 and payments-runbook.md:256-265 tell the owner to record disputes by hand from Moyasar's emails and settlement files; neither says to answer them. https://moyasar.com/en/resources/platform-terms-and-conditions/ (Last revised 14 April 2026), 7.2: 'shall respond to any inquiries or requests within a period not exceeding three (3) Business Days from the date of notification'; 9.1: the 200 SAR fee applies on 'Failure to provide a response or sufficient supporting documentation within three (3) Business Days from the date of receiving the information request'; 7.6: charges 'shall be deemed correct and final if no written objection is submitted within' 30 days of the statement. artifacts/acceptance/P08/moyasar-docs-2026-10-02.md:51 recorded only the 200 SAR. The order view shows entitlements but not download use (finance.download_tokens.uses, last_used_at), the evidence a digital 'not received' claim needs.
IMPACT: An owner following the docs waits for the twice-weekly settlement file and records the chargeback; by then the three business days have usually passed, so a winnable 'not received' claim on a downloaded book is lost: the amount is deducted and 200 SAR charged each time. A payout or fee difference recorded with «تسجيل فرق» but not objected to in writing within 30 days becomes final.
FIX: Add to operations.md 'Disputes and payouts' and the runbook: on Moyasar's chargeback email, answer within three business days with the order's evidence (order number, items, receipt, carrier and tracking, and for a digital item the download times from finance.download_tokens via a ready SQL query), then record it; object in writing within 30 days to any statement difference before recording it. Optionally show download count and last download per entitlement in the order view.

### VENDOR-PLAT-01 [medium] The owner's visit statistics ask Cloudflare for 7 days in one httpRequestsAdaptiveGroups query, but the dataset caps each query's time range by plan (a Free zone is reported at 1 day), and nothing checks or splits it
supabase/functions/_shared/analytics.ts:37 RUNTIME fix:small conf:medium prior:none: AUDIT-1/AUDIT-2 and operations.md cover maxPageSize, the content-type filter and sampling, not the per-query time range
EVIDENCE: analytics.ts:37 `const WINDOW_DAYS = 7`; analyticsWindow() builds one [now-7d, now) range used by both VISITS_QUERY and TOP_PATHS_QUERY (lines 75-105, 108-113); any GraphQL `errors` entry returns {status:'unavailable', reason:'GRAPHQL_ERROR'} (lines 258-260). Cloudflare: 'Each data node has its limits, such as ... the maximum time period (in seconds) that can be requested in one query' and 'Node limits are tied to requested zoneTag ... Higher plans ... can query over broader historical intervals' (https://developers.cloudflare.com/analytics/graphql-api/limits/, updated Oct 2, 2026); the per-zone value is the settings node's `maxDuration` (https://developers.cloudflare.com/analytics/graphql-api/features/discovery/settings/; its sample shows 259200 s = 3 days for an adaptive node). A third-party integration tested against standard zones reports httpRequestsAdaptiveGroups 'rejects any query window wider than 1 day ... cannot request a time range wider than 1d, but your query time range spans 1w' (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/urdigitalau/mcp-integrations/main/packages/cloudflare/README.md). docs/operations.md:518-523 tells the operator to confirm only edgeResponseContentTypeName and maxPageSize at E11, not maxDuration.
IMPACT: On the anas.studio zone (Cloudflare Free, docs/costs.md) every stats refresh can answer a GraphQL time-range error, so «الزيارات» and «أكثر الصفحات طلبًا» on /admin/stats show «غير متاح: ردّ الإحصاءات يحتوي على خطأ.» for ever. The owner never gets the traffic numbers the stats screen (P06) was built for, and E11's checklist would not tell him why. Fails safe (no invented numbers): data is absent, not wrong.
FIX: Read `settings { httpRequestsAdaptiveGroups { maxDuration notOlderThan maxPageSize } }` once per isolate (or at E11 and record it), and when maxDuration is below 7 days split the window into maxDuration-sized slices: sum `sum.visits` across slices and merge top-path counts by path before filtering and taking the top 10 (7 one-day slices = 14 queries per 5-minute cache, far inside 300 queries per 5 minutes). Add maxDuration to the E11 check in docs/operations.md:518-523 and PLANS/ARCHITECTURE.md:105.
PROOF: With the E11 least-privilege token on the anas.studio zone: POST https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/graphql {query: 'query($z:string){viewer{zones(filter:{zoneTag:$z}){settings{httpRequestsAdaptiveGroups{enabled maxDuration notOlderThan maxPageSize}}}}}', variables:{z:<zone id>}}. Wrong result: maxDuration below 604800. Then run VISITS_QUERY from analytics.ts with a 7-day range: wrong result is an errors[] entry such as 'cannot request a time range wider than 1d', and the admin stats action answering visits unavailable GRAPHQL_ERROR.

### VENDOR-PLAT-06 [medium] The runbook's restore into a new hosted project never redeploys the Edge Functions or rebuilds the site against the new project, so its own contact-form smoke test fails, or passes against the old project
docs/operations.md:1332 fix:small conf:high prior:same wave, found independently: ARCH-09, SUPPLY-07, OPS-PRIVACY-01 (restore runbook); wave A EF-ADMIN-01 (a restored project has no legacy keys)
EVIDENCE: docs/operations.md:1332-1408, 'A real restore into a new hosted project', steps 1-9: extract, create the project and psql-restore, Vault, pg_cron, `supabase secrets set`, Auth settings, Storage objects, deletion ledger, cleanup. No step runs `supabase functions deploy` against the new project, changes the Pages build variables or rebuilds the site, or re-registers the Resend webhook endpoint. Supabase's own guide for moving to a new project has a separate 'Migrating edge functions' section ending in `supabase functions deploy --project-ref your_target_project_ref` (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/migrating-within-supabase/backup-restore.md, fetched 2026-10-07). A new project has a new ref and new API keys, and the static export inlines the old ones at build time: ContactForm.tsx:107 `fetch(`${process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL}/functions/v1/contact``, likewise AvailabilityForm.tsx:41, quote.ts:318, orders.ts:102, the admin client (src/lib/supabase/browser.ts:13-14) and every library image (media-ref.ts:15-16, the old project's Storage host). Step 5 ends: 'finish with a smoke test: send one real message through the contact form from the site. It must answer «received» (201)'.
IMPACT: After a disaster restore the owner follows the runbook and then: if the old project is gone, the site's forms, admin sign-in and every library image fail and the runbook gives no next step; if the old project still exists (restoring after corruption or a bad migration), the smoke test succeeds against the OLD project, so the owner declares the restore done while contact messages, sign-ups, orders and staff sign-ins keep going to the abandoned database. The new project has no functions, so its pg_cron jobs (step 4) call 404s and nothing is mailed or reconciled.
FIX: Add the missing steps before the smoke test: `supabase link --project-ref <new>` then `supabase functions deploy` (all nine, with config.toml's verify_jwt settings); set the Pages project's NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL and NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY to the new project and run a production build (and, once SITE-STATE-1 exists, the gate's two variables); re-point the Resend webhook endpoint to the new functions URL (new RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET) and Google's OAuth redirect URI if Google sign-in is on. Make the smoke test confirm the request goes to the new ref.

### ARCH-04 [low] P11's Pages setup line names the build command `pnpm build`, while I40 needs `pnpm build && pnpm check:export` and the Pages Next.js preset fills in `npx next build`, which skips both the demo guard and the I39 fetch-cache clear
PLANS/WORK-PACKAGES.md:139 fix:trivial conf:high prior:I40 (open; this is the P11 line contradicting it), I39 (closed)
EVIDENCE: WORK-PACKAGES.md:139: 'a Cloudflare Pages project connected to the repository (build `pnpm build`, output `out` ...)'. ISSUES.md:29 (I40): 'the Pages build command must be `pnpm build && pnpm check:export` (or the deploy must gate on it) for the check to block a real deploy'. package.json:12: `build` = rmSync('.next/cache/fetch-cache') then `next build` (I39: a build reused an earlier build's responses from .next/cache/fetch-cache, artifacts/acceptance/P07/commands.txt:165-176). Cloudflare's guide for a static Next.js site sets the preset 'Next.js (Static HTML Export)' with 'Build command | npx next build' (https://developers.cloudflare.com/pages/framework-guides/nextjs/deploy-a-static-nextjs-site/), and Pages build caching restores `.next/cache` for Next.js once enabled (https://developers.cloudflare.com/pages/configuration/build-caching/).
IMPACT: An operator who follows P11 literally deploys without the demo-row check, so a hosted build that still shows «تجريبي» rows goes live; one who accepts the preset's `npx next build` also skips the fetch-cache clear, and with build caching on every later rebuild can serve the previous build's data (I39's stale catalog) while the owner home says the build succeeded.
FIX: Make WORK-PACKAGES P11 (and docs/deployment.md when written) say: Framework preset None, build command `pnpm build && pnpm check:export`, output `out`, and 'never `npx next build`'; or move the fetch-cache clear into a `prebuild` script so any `next build` invocation through pnpm clears it.

### ARCH-05 [low] Every Pages deployment stays publicly reachable at its own hash URL, so a post archived for a legal reason (or superseded policy text, or a pre-launch build) is never really unpublished; no plan step restricts or deletes old deployments
PLANS/WORK-PACKAGES.md:139 RUNTIME fix:trivial conf:medium
EVIDENCE: archive_document deletes the published row and requests a rebuild (20260930140000_audit2_fixes.sql:204-229), so only the next deployment lacks the post. Cloudflare: a deployment's 'unique, hash-based address ... <hash>.<project>.pages.dev ... These are atomic and may always be visited in the future' and 'By default, these deployment URLs are public'; 'Enable access policy' restricts the hash URLs (not the custom domain); old deployments are removed with `wrangler pages deployment delete` (https://developers.cloudflare.com/pages/configuration/preview-deployments/). Rollbacks: 'Any production deployment that has been successfully built is a valid rollback target' (https://developers.cloudflare.com/pages/configuration/rollbacks/). With D32 every publish is a new deployment. Neither P11 nor SITE-STATE-CONTRACT:51 (which names only the project's pages.dev alias) covers the per-deployment URLs.
IMPACT: A post Anas withdraws after a complaint, an old price or policy text, or the soon-period build stays reachable at every earlier deployment URL anyone kept (a shared preview link, a commit status link, a browser history entry), outside the SITE-STATE gate; the takedown is incomplete and nobody knows how to finish it.
FIX: Add to P11's Pages setup 'Enable access policy' (Settings > General) so hash deployment URLs require a Cloudflare login, and to docs/operations.md a takedown procedure: archive the post; if it must go at once, block the path with a zone rule on anas.studio until the rebuild lands; after the rebuild, list the deployments and delete those that contain the withdrawn page (wrangler pages deployment list, then wrangler pages deployment delete <id> --project-name <name>). Prove on the hosted preview that a production deployment's hash URL is covered by the policy.
PROOF: After two production deployments on the hosted project: `curl -sI https://<older-hash>.<project>.pages.dev/journal/<archived-slug>` answers 200 with the archived post (wrong result: a public page). After enabling the access policy the same request must redirect to Cloudflare Access login.

### ARCH-06 [low] The P10 CSP written in DATA-AND-SECURITY omits frame-src for Turnstile and img-src for the Supabase Storage origin; implemented as written it blocks every form's challenge and every library image
PLANS/DATA-AND-SECURITY.md:101 fix:trivial conf:high prior:wave A CLIENT-SEC plan gap (admin connect-src, /admin policy joining) covers a different part of the same line
EVIDENCE: DATA-AND-SECURITY.md:101 specifies `script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' https://challenges.cloudflare.com`, `style-src`, `worker-src 'self'` and `connect-src` for the Data API and /functions/v1, and no frame-src or img-src. Cloudflare: Turnstile needs 'script-src: https://challenges.cloudflare.com' and 'frame-src: https://challenges.cloudflare.com' (https://developers.cloudflare.com/turnstile/reference/content-security-policy/). Library images load from `${NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL}/storage/v1/object/public/media-public` (src/lib/media-ref.ts:123-125), a different origin from the site, and the public checkout, contact and notify forms render the Turnstile widget. The no-nonce form itself is right for a static export (Next.js: nonces need dynamic rendering; without nonces the guide uses `script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`, https://nextjs.org/docs/app/guides/content-security-policy).
IMPACT: A P10 policy with `default-src 'self'` built from this list makes the Turnstile iframe fail on /contact, /checkout and the availability form (no token, every submission refused) and leaves every image Anas uploaded blank; a hosted-preview check would catch it, but only after a round is spent.
FIX: Add to DATA-AND-SECURITY's CSP line `frame-src https://challenges.cloudflare.com` and `img-src 'self' data: blob: <SUPABASE_URL origin>` (plus `media-src` for the Storage origin if I33 moves the films there), and keep the admin's own needs from CLIENT-SEC's plan gap.

### ARCH-07 [low] The paid-file path accepts up to 100 MiB, but a Free Supabase project cannot take any upload over 50 MB; the plan leaves the hosted limit as an open check
supabase/config.toml:121 fix:trivial conf:high
EVIDENCE: config.toml:119-122: '100 MiB: the size of the largest paid file ... The hosted project's own global upload limit is set in its dashboard and is checked at P11', `file_size_limit = "100MiB"`; the paid-files bucket is created with file_size_limit 104857600 (payment_core.sql:408-409) and the admin pre-check allows 104,857,600 bytes (src/lib/admin-commerce.ts:102, 128); docs/operations.md:1234-1236 lists 'the hosted project's own limit and a real 100 MiB upload' as a hosted check. Supabase: 'For Free projects, the limit can't exceed 50 MB' (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/storage/uploads/file-limits), and a bucket limit 'can't be higher than this global limit'.
IMPACT: While the hosted project is on Free (the plan's E02 sandbox proof runs there), a book file between 50 MB and 100 MiB passes the admin's own check and is then refused by Storage; the e-book purchase in the sandbox proof cannot deliver its file. On Pro (E07) it works.
FIX: Record the 50 MB Free ceiling in docs/operations.md's hosted checks and the payments runbook, and either upload the real paid file only after the move to Pro or keep the sandbox file under 50 MB; optionally let the admin check read the limit from the `status` action instead of the constant.

The build bakes each picture's alt into its media reference (src/lib/content.ts:94-95, 120-126 `formatMediaRef({... alt})`), and the public views read it back (src/content/media-notes.ts:49 `parseMediaRef(id)?.alt`, used by PostView, BuiltRoomView, ShelfRoomView and Story). site_build_request is called only by publish_version, archive_document, publish_due and catalog_request_build (grep of the migrations); no trigger on public.media requests a build.
IMPACT: Anas corrects or removes an image's description (a wrong name, a person who asked not to be named, an accessibility fix) and the admin accepts it, but screen-reader users and the page source keep the old text indefinitely on a quiet site; nothing says a publish is needed.

EVIDENCE: `client = createClient(url, key)` (browser.ts:21) keeps supabase-js's defaults, so the session (access and refresh token) is stored in window.localStorage of the page's origin; the admin is served from the same Pages output and origin as the public site (`/admin`, ARCHITECTURE.md:3, 11). The public pages load third-party script in page context (Turnstile's api.js on the form pages; the Cloudflare Web Analytics beacon if enabled, DATA-AND-SECURITY:101), and the planned CSP is `script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' https://challenges.cloudflare.com` (DATA-AND-SECURITY:101), which a static export cannot tighten with nonces (Next.js CSP guide). No code path renders unescaped HTML today (no dangerouslySetInnerHTML in src; the Lexical renderer is an allowlist).
IMPACT: Nothing is exploitable as built. The boundary is thin: one future injection or a compromised third-party script on any public page (a journal post, the store, the contact page), visited by an owner who is signed in to the admin in the same browser, yields the owner's refresh token, which keeps every aal1 power (EF-ADMIN's plan gap) without the TOTP.
FIX: Serve the admin from its own hostname (for example admin.anas.studio as a second custom domain of the same Pages project, with /admin on the public host redirected there and the SITE-STATE gate exempting that host), so the staff session sits in a different origin's storage; add the host to the Auth redirect list. Decide in P10 with the CSP work.

### FIX-A1-05 [low] AUDIT-1 G4.6 half fixed: the graft hook helper still falls back to importing <project>/dist/claude/hooks.js
.claude/helpers/graft-hooks.cjs:64 fix:trivial conf:high prior:AUDIT-1 G4.6 (recorded fixed by SCRIPTS)
EVIDENCE: entry(): `return path.join(dir, 'dist', 'claude', name); // last-ditch` with `const dir = process.env.CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR || process.cwd()` (line 6), then line 67 `import(pathToFileURL(entry("hooks.js")).href)`. The settings half of G4.6 holds (no `node dist/cli.js` or `graft-dev` allow entries), but git log --all -- .claude/helpers/graft-hooks.cjs shows only the 8ffdb9c baseline: the hook change the fix prescribed was never committed
IMPACT: On a machine where graft is not found through the baked path, the project's node_modules, node's lib or the npm global root, every Claude Code hook in this repository imports and runs <repo>/dist/claude/hooks.js without a prompt; dist/ is not git-ignored, so a file committed there by a branch or a tool would run in the owner's sessions. Exploitability is low while the global graft install exists
FIX: Make the last-ditch branch return null and guard the import (`const e = entry('hooks.js'); if (e) import(...)`), in both helpers; since graft regenerates helper files on a version mismatch, also record it in the graft pin note so a re-install does not bring the fallback back

### FIX-A1-06 [low] AUDIT-1 S04.1's costs note went stale with P08's three tiers: availability notices now stop at 50, not 80
docs/costs.md:13 fix:trivial conf:high prior:AUDIT-1 S04.1 (its doc updates); stale since c04525f (P08 round 5)
EVIDENCE: costs.md:13 "The outbox holds back the last 20 of each day for receipts (priority 0): contact and availability notices stop at 80". Since 20261002120000_order_emails.sql:323 the claim is `v_sent_today < p_daily_quota - case o.priority when 0 then 0 when 1 then p_reserve else p_reserve + p_low_reserve end`; availability and notify_confirm are priority 2 (20261002150000_notifications.sql:117,380) and outbox.ts sets LOW_RESERVE = 30, so they stop at 100 - 20 - 30 = 50; order_link (priority 1) is new and absent from the note
IMPACT: The owner reading the costs page for the Resend Free plan expects availability mail to keep going to 80 a day and reads a backlog after 50 as a fault; the page is the one the owner uses to judge whether to buy a paid Resend plan
FIX: Rewrite the Resend row's note to the three tiers: priority 0 (receipts, shipping, refunds, ready, owner alerts) to 100, priority 1 (contact notices, order-link recoveries, at most 20 links a day) to 80, priority 2 (confirmations, at most 30 a day, and availability notices) to 50; sign-in codes outside the outbox

### FIX-A2-01 [low] Soon/maintenance design B still scrolls 4-17 px at 520-700 px widths: the word's height budget assumes the smallest clamps
artifacts/soon/site/v/b/style.css:49 fix:trivial conf:high prior:MAINT-1 (design B fits short screens); EXECUTION-STATUS SOON-1 row records only design C's 52 px at 320x568
EVIDENCE: `--F: max(96px, min((100vw - 32px) / 1.676, (var(--h) - 405px) / 1.3));` reserves a fixed 405px for everything but the word, while five vertical clamps grow with the viewport height (.sky padding clamp(20px, h*0.036, 32px), .ground --ft clamp(20px, h*0.038, 30px) and --fb clamp(18px, h*0.044, 34px), .signature margin clamp(10px, h*0.028, 22px), .domain margin clamp(8px, h*0.02, 16px): about 94px at 568 high, about 131px at 800 high). Rendered from disk with reduced motion: /v/b/ and its twin /m/b/ (same stylesheet) give pageH 770 at 520x760, 724 at 540x720, 815 at 600x800, 917 at 700x900; they fit at 320x568, 360x640, 480x800, 600x960 and 760x1000. The MAINT-1 fix «design B fits short screens» and EXECUTION-STATUS's «one screen from 360 wide» therefore do not hold where the word is height-limited (portrait windows between about 500 and 780 px wide). The site files are unchanged since 0b915c5, so the live domain shows the same.
IMPACT: A visitor on a small tablet, a foldable's inner screen or a narrow desktop window sees a page that scrolls by up to 17 px, with the domain line under the fold, on a page designed as one still screen; cosmetic, no content lost.
FIX: Reserve the clamps' upper bound in the height term, for example `(var(--h) - 441px) / 1.3`, or subtract the same clamp() expressions instead of the 405px constant; re-run shots.cjs at 540x720, 600x800 and 700x900.
PROOF: MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1 node C:/Users/alazi/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-alazi-Downloads-Tech-Code-My-projects-not-shipped-yet-ANASAQ-ME/e4be5ed4-f198-443e-9131-354d6dec169a/scratchpad/audit/FIX-A2/soon-render.mjs /v/b/,/m/b/ 520x760,540x720,600x800,700x900 (renders artifacts/soon/site from disk, no server): pageH exceeds the viewport height.

### FIX-A2-02 [low] A contact-form flood fills the whole priority-1 mail band P08 now shares with order_link, holding every order-recovery link to the next UTC day
supabase/migrations/20261002120000_order_emails.sql:323 RUNTIME fix:small conf:medium prior:AUDIT-2 DB-contacts-2 (the 40-a-day cap sized to the band); P08 R5-01 (claim-level contact cap removed); not raised in round 5's audit or rulings, nor in wave A
EVIDENCE: AUDIT-2's DB-contacts-2 fix sized contact_submit's daily cap to the band: «40 a day is at most 80 notices with two recipients» (20260930140000_audit2_fixes.sql:414-422, `rate_limit_take('contact:all', …, 40, interval '1 day')`). P08 then put `order_link` into the same tier (P08-CONTRACT.md section 8: priority 1 = contact_notice and order_link, sent while the day's sends are under 80), and round 5's R5-01 ruling removed any claim-level contact cap. The claim gates by the day's total and orders FIFO: `and v_sent_today < p_daily_quota - case o.priority when 0 then 0 when 1 then p_reserve else …` (:323) / `order by o.priority, o.next_at, o.id` (:327); contact notices are queued one per active owner or operations member (audit2_fixes.sql:457-462) and order links at priority 1 (20261002140000_delivery.sql:253). With two recipients, 40 Turnstile-passing messages queue 80 notices; the dispatcher claims up to CLAIM_LIMIT 10 a minute (supabase/functions/_shared/outbox.ts:65), so the 80-send band is spent within minutes and no priority-1 row is claimed again until 00:00 UTC.
IMPACT: Precondition: two or more active owner/operations recipients and someone willing to pass Turnstile 40 times a day (5 an hour per IP, 3 an hour per address, 40 a day in total). Then every buyer who asks «استعادة رابط الطلب» that day (the only way back to a paid order and its download once the mailed 7-day link has expired) gets the link only after midnight UTC, and the same happens each day the flood repeats; real visitors' contact notices wait too. Bounded to about a day per message; no money or data is lost.
FIX: Keep part of the band for the recovery links: let contact notices claim only while the day's sends are under p_daily_quota - p_reserve - 20 (or under the band minus the links still allowed today, 20 - v_link_today), so order_link's own 20-a-day cap always has room; add a two-recipient case to tests/integration/order-emails.test.ts (80 notices sent today, an order link is still claimed). Alternatively move order_link to priority 0, since it is capped at 20 a day.
PROOF: On a fresh local stack, in one transaction rolled back at the end: move today's sent rows out of the day (update finance.email_outbox set sent_at = now() - interval '2 days' where sent_at >= date_trunc('day', now(), 'UTC')); insert 80 rows kind 'contact_notice', priority 1, status 'pending', next_at now() - interval '1 hour' (recipients = active owners' addresses), and 1 row kind 'order_link', priority 1, status 'pending', next_at now(); loop: select * from public.outbox_claim(1, 120, 100, 20, 3000, 30); for each returned id update finance.email_outbox set status = 'sent', sent_at = now() where id = <id>; stop when nothing is returned. Wrong result: the loop stops after 80 contact notices and the order_link row is still 'pending' (it would be claimed only after 00:00 UTC).

### FIX-A2-04 [low] The availability sweep's switch-off guard ignores the other closures: a restock during a policy-approval reset mails subscribers who cannot buy, and spends their one notice
supabase/migrations/20261002150000_notifications.sql:352 fix:trivial conf:high prior:P08 round 8, the orchestrator's own finding (sweep while the switch is off); AUDIT-2 I48 (2)
EVIDENCE: Round 8's orchestrator fix («the sweep told subscribers an item was back while checkout was switched off … It now does nothing at all while the switch is off») checks the stored flag only: `if not coalesce((select s.checkout_enabled from finance.commerce_settings s where s.id = 1), false) then return 0;`. The buyers' gate is wider: checkout_quote and checkout_create also close for an unset seller or an empty policy_revisions (20261002110000_checkout_payment.sql:352-356, :453-461). A subscriber is told once per revision: the sweep sets notified_revision = revision when it queues the mail (:369-374), and the revision moves only on a false→true flip. PLANS/SITE-STATE-CONTRACT.md plans one more condition (site_state = 'open') but not the readiness ones.
IMPACT: While the approval is reset (any republish of an approved policy, FIX-A2-03) a restocked variant still sends «توفّر» mails; the subscriber finds «الشراء غير متاح حاليًا»; when the owner re-approves, the variant is still sellable, the revision does not move and nobody is told again, so the sign-up's promise is spent on a moment nothing could be bought: the case round 8 fixed for the switch.
FIX: Gate the sweep on the same readiness the quote uses: `checkout_enabled and seller_legal_name is not null and seller_registration is not null and policy_revisions <> '{}'::jsonb` (one select of the settings row); add a case to tests/integration/notify.test.ts with the approval emptied.

### OPS-PRIVACY-04 [low] No runbook step for a leaked order link (the code cannot revoke one, wave A DB-POST-03)
docs/operations.md:797 fix:small conf:high prior:Code side reported as DB-POST-03 (medium) and EF-ACCESS-13 (low) in wave A; this finding is only the missing runbook procedure the slice asks for.
EVIDENCE: The order link token is HMAC(pepper, 'order-access:' + idempotencyKey [+ version]) (tokens.ts:43-45). The only writer of `access_token_version` is `order_recover_apply`, and only for an expired link: "A working link is never replaced: with no hash the order keeps its token and only its expiry is renewed" (20261002140000_delivery.sql:202-205, 236-244). grep of the migrations for access_token_version shows no other writer; grep of docs for 'leak' finds no procedure; the admin offers no control. operations.md:797-803 documents only a lost link.
IMPACT: A buyer who forwards the receipt mail or posts a screenshot of the link (or whose mailbox someone else reads) cannot have it stopped: for up to 7 days, renewed whenever the buyer asks for recovery, the holder sees the order's lines, carrier and tracking number, files return requests, and mints download tokens for the paid book (3 downloads each, 10 tokens a file a day). The owner can only wait.
FIX: Once DB-POST-03 adds a rotate/revoke function, add an operations.md entry under "What the buyer has": how the owner kills a leaked link (the function, or until then the SQL `update finance.orders set access_token_expires_at = now() where order_number = <n>` run as the migration role and noted in the audit), and that the buyer then asks for a new link on /orders, which mints the next version the old link cannot derive.

### OPS-PRIVACY-06 [low] The plan says the token pepper is rotated by version; the code has one unversioned pepper whose change kills every link
PLANS/DATA-AND-SECURITY.md:75 fix:trivial conf:high prior:EF-ACCESS-04 (recovery after a pepper change) and EF-ACCESS-08 (pepper length) are code-side neighbours; the plan-line mismatch is not in them.
EVIDENCE: DATA-AND-SECURITY.md:75: "Token hashes use a server pepper; rotate by version." supabase/functions/_shared/tokens.ts:3-4: "All of them are keyed by `TOKEN_HASH_PEPPER`, so it is never rotated casually: a new value kills every link already mailed." No pepper version is stored on orders, download tokens or notifications (access_token_version is the per-order recovery version), and operations.md:1384-1392 tells the operator to reuse the original pepper.
IMPACT: An operator or a later package reading the plan believes the pepper can be rotated after a leak; doing so 404s every order, download, confirmation and unsubscribe link and resets every throttle key mid-window.
FIX: Correct the plan line to what is built (one pepper, not rotatable without re-issuing links), or add a pepper key id to the stored hashes and token MACs; the first is a one-line edit.

### OPS-PRIVACY-07 [low] A sign-up purged after 7 or 30 days leaves its address in the sent confirmation and availability mail rows for ever; no job purges sent outbox rows
supabase/migrations/20261002150000_notifications.sql:426 RUNTIME fix:small conf:high prior:none (DB-OPS-03/EF-ACCESS-05 are about the purge timing of pending rows, not the sent mail)
EVIDENCE: notifications_purge deletes the notification and only its unsent mail: `delete from finance.email_outbox o where o.kind in ('notify_confirm', 'availability') and o.payload ->> 'notificationId' in (select g.id::text from gone g) and o.status not in ('sent', 'sending')` (lines 426-430). The six `delete from finance.email_outbox` statements in the migrations are the two staff/contact erasures, contacts_purge (contact notices only), notify_unsubscribe and this purge (unsent only), and privacy_buyer_erase; none removes a sent notify_confirm/availability row. The P06 table comment still says "Purging old sent rows is round-3 backup/retention work" (20260926120000_contacts_and_email.sql:121-123). privacy-data-map.md:24 tells the reader a pending sign-up goes 7 days after its last request and an unsubscribed one 30 days after it unsubscribed. The map's outbox row (line 11) does say no job purges a sent receipt or notice row and that it keeps 'the buyer's address until an erase request redacts it', but it frames this as a buyer and E08 question; a sign-up who never bought or confirmed has no reason to file an erase request.
IMPACT: A person whose address a stranger typed into «أخبرني عند توفره» and who never confirmed (or who unsubscribed) is gone from public.notifications on schedule, but the recipient column of each confirmation and availability mail sent to them keeps the address in finance.email_outbox and in every later backup, with no end date and no purpose (the 5-in-30-days cap reads only the last 30 days). A privacy policy written from the map would state 7 and 30 days and be untrue; only an erase request the person has no reason to make removes it.
FIX: In finance.notifications_purge (or a new daily step) delete sent notify_confirm and availability rows older than 30 days, which the confirmation cap no longer reads; and say in the map's outbox row which sent kinds are kept and for how long (receipts and other buyer mail stay an E08 question).
PROOF: Local stack: notify_subscribe for x@example.com on an out-of-stock variant, run the outbox (the notify_confirm row becomes sent), then `update public.notifications set updated_at = now() - interval '8 days' where email = 'x@example.com'; select finance.notifications_purge(); select count(*) from finance.email_outbox where recipient = 'x@example.com';` Expected wrong result: the notification row is gone and the count is 1.

### OPS-PRIVACY-17 [low] The privacy runbook accepts a message 'written from' an address as proof of identity, which a forged From header satisfies, before an irreversible erase
docs/privacy-data-map.md:40 fix:trivial conf:high prior:none
EVIDENCE: 'Verify the person first. A visitor proves they own the address by writing from it, or by answering a message sent to it.' (privacy-data-map.md:40). The same check gates `privacy_buyer_erase` and `privacy_erase_contacts`, which delete notification rows, unsent mail, erasable orders and the customer profile and cannot be undone except from a backup (lines 48, 86).
IMPACT: Anyone can send a mail whose From is another person's address (help@anas.studio is forwarded to Gmail by Email Routing, which shows the claimed sender); following the first option, the owner erases that person's sign-ups, unpaid orders and profile, and redacts their mail history, on a stranger's request. An export is safe (it is sent to the address), an erase is not.
FIX: For an erase, require the second option: send a short message to the address and act only on the reply (or on a request that arrives from the address and is then confirmed by a reply); keep 'writing from it' for exports only.

### SUPPLY-04 [low] The backup passphrase is not Unicode-normalized before scrypt, so a visually identical Arabic passphrase can fail to open the backup
scripts/lib/backup-format.mjs:71 fix:small conf:medium
EVIDENCE: Line 71 `return scryptSync(passphrase, salt, 32, SCRYPT)` hashes the string as typed; `grep -rn normalize( scripts/` finds nothing. passphrase.mjs:1-6 explicitly supports Arabic passphrases (`the same keystrokes open the backup later`) and keeps every non-control code point, combining marks and U+200C/U+200F included.
IMPACT: Arabic has canonically equivalent spellings that render identically: shadda+fatha typed in either order (U+0651 U+064E vs U+064E U+0651), أ as U+0623 or U+0627 U+0654 (an IME, a password manager or another OS that normalizes). A restore on another machine (runbook step 1: any machine) can refuse the right passphrase with WRONG_PASSPHRASE_OR_DAMAGED, and there is no reset: the backups are lost.
FIX: Derive from `passphrase.normalize('NFC')` in a format version 2 (version 1 keeps the raw string, or try raw then NFC when reading), and say in docs/operations.md that the passphrase is compared after NFC.

### SUPPLY-06 [low] No .gitattributes: on a Windows clone with Git's default core.autocrlf=true, check:frozen reports three frozen files as modified
.gitattributes:1 fix:trivial conf:high
EVIDENCE: The repository has no .gitattributes. `git ls-files --eol deploy` shows deploy/mobile-fix.css, deploy/design/support.js and deploy/design/index.html stored i/lf; check-frozen.mjs:95-97 hashes raw working-tree bytes. This machine passes only because `git config core.autocrlf` is false; Git for Windows installs with autocrlf=true, which checks those files out as CRLF.
IMPACT: On a re-clone on another Windows machine, `pnpm check:frozen` (part of `pnpm check` and the acceptance routine) fails with `modified: deploy/...` although nothing changed; the tempting `node scripts/check-frozen.mjs --write` would record CRLF hashes and break CI on Linux.
FIX: Add a .gitattributes with `deploy/** -text` (byte-exact frozen sources), optionally `* text=auto eol=lf` for the rest.

### SUPPLY-08 [low] The inside-the-repository guard for --extract (and backup --out) lets through any folder whose name starts with two dots
scripts/restore-check.mjs:70 fix:trivial conf:high prior:AUDIT-1 S17.4 (guard added there; holds except this case)
EVIDENCE: Line 70 `if (relDest === '' || (!relDest.startsWith('..') && !isAbsolute(relDest)))` refuses only paths that do not start with `..`. Measured with node: path.win32.relative('C:/repo', 'C:/repo/..plain') is `..plain`, so the guard does not block it (posix behaves the same). backup.mjs:67 uses the same test for --out.
IMPACT: `pnpm restore-check <file> --extract ..plain` from the repository root writes the decrypted dumps (auth data, TOTP secrets, every customer record) into a folder inside the repository that .gitignore does not cover, the case AUDIT-1 S17.4 meant to refuse; a later `git add -A` publishes them. Needs an unusual folder name.
FIX: Treat a path as outside only when `relDest === '..' || relDest.startsWith('..' + sep)` or it is absolute (sep from node:path), at restore-check.mjs:70 and backup.mjs:67.

### SUPPLY-09 [low] restore-check never checks that the scratch stack stopped, so a failed or cut-off stop leaves the restored plaintext database in a Docker volume with no message
scripts/restore-check.mjs:401 fix:trivial conf:high
EVIDENCE: cleanup() line 401: `supabase(['stop', '--no-backup', '--workdir', scratch], { stdio: 'ignore', timeout: 300000 })`, result discarded, then rmSync(scratch). Lines 397-398 note that `supabase stop` can take minutes, longer than Windows allows after the console closes (the SIGHUP handler, line 512, only calls process.exit). The header (lines 21-22) promises the scratch stack `is stopped with --no-backup` on the way out.
IMPACT: When Docker is slow, the stop times out, or the window is closed mid-rehearsal, the scratch database volume keeps an unencrypted copy of the whole production database (auth.users, auth.mfa_factors, finance) on the owner's disk until a later rehearsal happens to clear it, and nothing tells him.
FIX: Check the stop result; when it is non-zero or timed out, print that the scratch stack may still hold the restored data and the command `supabase stop --no-backup --project-id anasaq-restore-check`, and exit non-zero.

IMPACT: The 'stays the owner's alone' claim for a caller with no session is not pinned: a 500 from a function that threw on a missing session, or a 502/503 from the gateway, passes. The same file's line 192 and payment-http.test.ts:956 use the strict form.
FIX: expect([401, 403]).toContain((await adminCall(null, { action: 'stats' })).status)

### TEST-DB-04 [low] The local-only guard checks only that DATABASE_URL is loopback; nothing ties it to the stack `supabase status` reports, and three spec files never consult that stack
src/lib/env.ts:38 fix:small conf:high
EVIDENCE: assertLocalTestDatabase() requires TEST_ENV === 'local' and a host in LOCAL_HOSTS ('localhost', '127.0.0.1', '::1', '[::1]', 'host.docker.internal') (env.ts:38-51, _shared/env.ts:57). support.ts applies the loopback rule to the API host only (support.ts:1-4, 26-34); its pg helpers use `process.env.DATABASE_URL ?? 'postgresql://postgres:postgres@127.0.0.1:54322/postgres'` (support.ts:173, 188, 243, 406) without comparing it to status.DB_URL. buyer-retention.test.ts, cron-jobs.test.ts and static-site.test.ts do not import support.ts (grep -L "from './support'").
IMPACT: Any loopback Postgres passes: another project's local stack on 54322 (the case on this machine today) or a port-forward to a hosted database. Those three files write only inside rolled-back transactions, so no data loss was found; but a mixed run (API from this stack, SQL to another database) reports on the wrong database, and the helpers never refuse on their own.
FIX: In support.ts read status.DB_URL and throw unless DATABASE_URL has the same host and port; replace the hard-coded fallbacks with the checked URL; import the same check in the three files that skip support.ts.

### TEST-DB-05 [low] outbox.test.ts re-dates every other pending, uncertain or sending mail in the shared database and never restores it
tests/integration/outbox.test.ts:67 fix:small conf:high prior:R2-08 (artifacts/acceptance/P08/rounds/round-02.md:84, payment.test.ts parkAll; recorded, not changed). This copy also rewrites first_attempt_at.
EVIDENCE: parkOthers(): `update finance.email_outbox set next_at = now() + interval '1 day', first_attempt_at = now() - interval '2 days' where dedupe_key not like $1 and status in ('pending', 'uncertain', 'sending')`, committed and called by 17 tests; afterAll (29-32) restores nothing. email-delivery.test.ts:239-245 does the same to every foreign due row; order-emails.test.ts:1129 pushes every other pending/uncertain row a day out (next_at only).
IMPACT: Foreign uncertain/sending rows are pushed past the 23-hour idempotency window (first_attempt_at two days ago), so the claim never retries them and they need a person's duplicate-risk replay; pending mail of a dev session or e2e run is delayed a day; which rows are hit depends on file order.
FIX: Record the ids and old (next_at, first_attempt_at) of parked rows and restore them in afterAll, or park only rows created before the file started and only next_at.

No test in tests/integration, tests/unit or tests/e2e updates or deletes an audit row, so the trigger never fires in the suite; disputes have the equivalent test (stats.test.ts:1103).

### TEST-DB-07 [low] No test presents a tampered JWT: the owner step-up (aal2, fresh TOTP) that guards refunds, disputes and the commerce settings is proven only with genuine tokens
tests/integration/disputes-http.test.ts:176 fix:small conf:high
EVIDENCE: The step-up lives only in the Edge Functions (admin.ts:225-248 `if (!staff.recentTotp) ...`; SQL cannot see aal/amr), and staffFromRequest (supabase/functions/_shared/staff.ts:36) trusts `client.auth.getClaims(token)`. Every refusal test uses a real session: a stale owner (disputes-http.test.ts:186, refunds-http.test.ts:758), editor/operations, no bearer, or the literal 'not-a-jwt' (stats.test.ts:116, in-process). No test anywhere re-signs or edits a JWT.
IMPACT: If verify_jwt were turned off for admin or staffFromRequest changed to decode without verifying, a token whose payload was edited to aal 'aal2' with a fresh 'totp' amr would move money and nothing in test:db or e2e would notice. Today both layers verify (config.toml:449-453; getClaims per the vendor docs), so no exploit exists.
FIX: Take a real aal1 owner token, rewrite its payload (aal 'aal2', amr [{method:'totp', timestamp: now}]) keeping the old signature, and also sign one with a random HS256 key; send both to admin dispute-record over HTTP and to handleAdmin in-process; expect 401 and nothing written.
PROOF: POST /functions/v1/admin {action:'dispute-record', ...} with the edited token: expected 401 (201/200 means the step-up is bypassable).
