### TEST-E2E-03 [low] The team screen silently drops the newest staff past 1,000 rows: the auth.spec failure on a polluted database is a product truncation, not only pollution
supabase/migrations/20260925090000_audit_and_staff_directory.sql:71 RUNTIME fix:trivial conf:high prior:lead:TEST-E2E-01 (confirmed; its line 76 is 71); HANDOFF.md:32; AUDIT-2 GAP-G1-10 (refuted/low, pollution angle only)
EVIDENCE: staff_directory() returns every staff row (active or not) `order by s.created_at;` (line 71, ascending); TeamView.tsx:43 calls `supabase.rpc('staff_directory')` with no range and no truncation note; supabase/config.toml:18 `max_rows = 1000` makes PostgREST cut the reply silently. auth.spec.ts:65 then expects the just-invited member. Contrast TableList.tsx:150-152, which says «تعرض القائمة أول 500 سجل.» when it truncates. The e2e helpers create about 45 staff per run and never remove them (helpers.ts:80-139); the harness only sets active=false.
IMPACT: With more than 1,000 staff rows the owner never sees the newest members on «الفريق», including one just invited, and nothing says the list is cut. Unreachable for the real studio's handful of staff, so the realistic effect is the recurring local acceptance failure that HANDOFF.md:32 and docs/development.md:338-343 record as test pollution.
FIX: Order staff_directory by active desc, created_at desc (or page it, as TableList does, with a note when cut), and have the e2e helpers retire the users they create.
PROOF: On a local DB insert 1,001 auth users with staff rows, then POST /rest/v1/rpc/staff_directory with an owner JWT: the reply has 1,000 rows and lacks the newest user_id.

### TEST-E2E-04 [low] Tests in store-admin.spec.ts and owner-operations.spec.ts read module variables that only an earlier test sets: none of them can run alone, and one failure cascades
tests/e2e/store-admin.spec.ts:104 fix:small conf:high prior:none
EVIDENCE: store-admin.spec.ts: `productId` and `variantId` are assigned inside test 1 (lines 104-106, 128) and read by the quote test (161 `lines: [{ variantId, quantity: 1 }]`), the create test (181-188), the stale-save test (215 `/admin/store/products/edit?id=${productId}`), the operations test (267) and the screenshots (376). owner-operations.spec.ts: `contactId` (153) and `noticeProviderId`/`noticeRecipient` (201-202) are set by tests 1 and 3 and read by tests 2 (168), 3 (194) and the webhook test (266); the home test says it itself (367: "The sweep test above recorded a run, so the daily job shows its status"). playwright.config.ts:36-39: one worker, file order, retries 0.
IMPACT: `playwright test -g "stale save"`, or any rerun of one failed test, fails on `id=undefined` instead of testing anything; when test 1 fails, Playwright starts a new worker, the module variables are undefined and the later tests fail with misleading errors that hide which behaviour broke. No false pass, but it multiplies reruns and sign-in mails during a battery.
FIX: Create the fixture product, variant and contact in a beforeAll (as the P08 blocks of the same files already do with seed()), and give the home test its own sweep run.

### TEST-E2E-05 [low] readCodeFromMailpit returns the newest mail without knowing whether it is the new one, so a second sign-in of the same address can submit the spent code
tests/e2e/helpers.ts:166 fix:trivial conf:medium prior:none
EVIDENCE: helpers.ts:163-172 searches `to:<email>`, takes `messages?.[0]` (Mailpit sorts search results by received date, descending: github.com/axllent/mailpit server/apiv1/messages.go:214) and returns its first `\b\d{6}\b` unless it equals `previousCode`. signInByCode (333-341) forwards previousCode only when a caller passes it. store-admin.spec.ts signs the same `owner.email` in five times without it (lines 89, 214, 236, 275, 370), cms.spec.ts twice (666, 677). SignIn.tsx:41-52 shows the same SENT sentence for every answer except a lost connection and a 429, so a masked failure (an SMTP or server error) also reads as sent.
IMPACT: When the new mail is not yet the newest message for the address (late hand-off to Mailpit) or was never sent (a masked non-429 failure), the helper returns the previous, already used code: the test fails at `toHaveURL(/\/admin$/)` with no hint that the code was stale, a flake that reads like a sign-in regression.
FIX: Before clicking «أرسل الرمز», record the ID of the newest message for the address and wait for a newer one; make signInByCode always do it instead of relying on callers to pass previousCode.

### TEST-UNIT-01 [low] Process-spawning tests (restore-check, backup-cli) run under the default 5 s timeout while their siblings get 30 s; the pdf.js test races its 15 s; the gate goes red on a loaded machine
tests/unit/restore-check.test.ts:51 fix:trivial conf:high prior:lead:TEST-UNIT-01 and lead:TEST-UNIT-02. The brief asked whose fault the timeouts are: the tests'. They give a fixed 5 s to a Node process spawn and 15 s to a 2 MB library import plus font parsing on a starved machine. The code adds only the avoidable eager supabase-js import.
EVIDENCE: Seven tests in tests/unit/restore-check.test.ts spawn `node scripts/restore-check.mjs` with no per-test timeout (line 51, line 60 which spawns twice, and the five cases of the it.each at 67-78), and five in tests/unit/backup-cli.test.ts spawn scripts/backup.mjs the same way (line 45 and the four cases of the it.each at 51-62). vitest.config.ts sets no testTimeout, so each has 5000 ms, while the two scrypt tests in restore-check.test.ts pass 30_000. restore-check.mjs statically imports @supabase/supabase-js (line 31) before it parses argv (lines 48-58), so even --help pays that import. Measured on the owner's machine, idle: `node scripts/restore-check.mjs --help` took 1748-2511 ms, `node scripts/backup.mjs --help` 775-2002 ms, bare `node -e 0` 1119-1392 ms. In the baseline (artifacts/acceptance/FABLE-AUDIT/before/test.log), five restore-check tests failed with 'Test timed out in 5000ms' at 5742-9293 ms, and reader-mapping.test.ts:169 failed at 27442 ms against its 15_000 (idle here, the pdf.js import takes 1055-5168 ms and the parse 1414-2062 ms; fixLigatures is linear and is not the cause).
IMPACT: pnpm test, and pnpm check, which is the acceptance gate, fail on the owner's machine whenever it is loaded, for reasons unrelated to the code. A suite that is known to be red for no reason teaches the orchestrator to discount real failures. CI runs on an unloaded Linux runner and stays green, so the flake is only seen locally.
FIX: Give every spawn test the same explicit timeout as its siblings (e.g. `{ timeout: 30_000 }` on the restore-check describes at lines 50 and 59 and on backup-cli's describe at line 44), and give the pdf.js test 60_000. Optionally, move the supabase-js import in restore-check.mjs below the argument parsing (as a dynamic import) so --help and usage errors never load it.

### TEST-UNIT-02 [low] The stats test that claims 'only the analytics part stays cached' cannot fail: the clock is frozen and analytics is unconfigured
tests/unit/admin-function.test.ts:719 fix:small conf:high
EVIDENCE: beforeEach fakes Date at NOW and never advances it (lines 695-700). The test then asserts `expect(second.body.data.generatedAt).toBe(first.body.data.generatedAt)` and that `analytics` is equal (lines 727-728). ownerStats() builds `generatedAt: new Date().toISOString()` (stats.ts:16). With ANALYTICS_TOKEN unset, fetchAnalytics() returns the constant {status:'unavailable', reason:'NOT_CONFIGURED'} (analytics.ts:233). Both values are the same with or without the cache, so deleting the cache check at admin.ts:564 (`if (cachedStats && Date.now() - cachedStats.at < cachedStats.ttl) return ...`) or swapping the 5-minute and 30-second TTLs (admin.ts:570) leaves every unit test green.
IMPACT: The per-isolate analytics cache (5 min for a good answer, 30 s for a failure) has no working test. A regression that disables it sends two Cloudflare GraphQL queries every time the owner views stats (the dashboard and the owner home both read them). A regression that inverts the expiry shows the owner a stale or failed analytics answer indefinitely. The test name tells a reviewer the cache is proven.
FIX: In that test, configure analytics (ANALYTICS_TOKEN, CLOUDFLARE_ZONE_ID, SITE_URL) with a stubbed fetch that counts calls. Advance the faked clock between the two calls (after 1 minute: no new fetch; after 6 minutes: a second fetch; 31 s after an 'unavailable' answer: a refetch), and assert the fetch count, not generatedAt.

### TEST-UNIT-03 [low] '429 (quota / rate limit) is a retry' never sends a quota error, so the QUOTA classification the outbox stops on has no CI test
tests/unit/email.test.ts:109 fix:trivial conf:high
EVIDENCE: The test sends only `{ name: 'rate_limit_exceeded' }` with 429 and expects error 'RATE_LIMIT'. email.ts:62-64 maps `daily_quota_exceeded` and `monthly_quota_exceeded` to error 'QUOTA', and outbox.ts:281 stops the run only on `outcome.error === 'QUOTA'` (the SQL gives the attempt back only for QUOTA). No unit test sends either quota name (grep of tests/unit for daily_quota_exceeded finds none). The only coverage is tests/integration/email-delivery.test.ts:346, and CI runs `pnpm test` only (.github/workflows/ci.yml: 'Not run here ... pnpm test:db'). The names themselves match Resend's current errors page.
IMPACT: A one-line regression in the quota branch (e.g. comparing `error.name` instead of `name`, or a renamed constant) turns every quota refusal into RATE_LIMIT. The outbox then keeps claiming after Resend's daily cap, each row burns an attempt instead of waiting for the UTC reset, and receipts can reach 'exhausted' on a busy day. CI stays green; only a local test:db run would notice.
FIX: Add `daily_quota_exceeded` and `monthly_quota_exceeded` cases (429 -> {outcome:'retry', error:'QUOTA'}) to email.test.ts. Add one outbox.test.ts case where the first send answers 429 daily_quota_exceeded and the run stops after one claim, with outbox_result p_error 'QUOTA'.

### TEST-UNIT-04 [low] Nothing pins each form's Turnstile widget action to the action its function expects, and local keys skip the check
src/lib/turnstile.ts:47 fix:small conf:high prior:AUDIT-1 S20.6 added only the server-side assertions.
EVIDENCE: The four widgets each pass a literal: useTurnstile('contact') (ContactForm.tsx:52), useTurnstile('notify') (AvailabilityForm.tsx:83), useTurnstile('checkout') (CheckoutForm.tsx:158) and useTurnstile('order-recover') (OrderPage.tsx:306). The functions compare against separate literals (checkout.ts:433, contact.ts:133, notify.ts:142, orders.ts:190). The unit tests pin only the server side (checkout.test.ts:867, notify.test.ts:246, orders.test.ts:289, contact-route.test.ts:99). Every local run uses the always-pass test secret (scripts/local-env.mjs:49), for which verifyTurnstile returns ok before the action check (turnstile.ts:107-111), so the e2e suite cannot see a mismatch either.
IMPACT: Renaming the action on either side (for example a widget action changed in a UI edit) passes lint, the unit suite and the whole local e2e battery. On the hosted site, every real submission of that form is then refused with 400 «تعذّر التحقق من أنك إنسان.»: no checkout, no contact message, no order-link recovery. The break would surface only at the hosted P11 check or from buyers.
FIX: Export one constant per action from a shared module (e.g. TURNSTILE_ACTIONS = {contact, notify, checkout, recover} in supabase/functions/_shared/turnstile.ts) and use it on both sides. Alternatively, add a unit test that reads the useTurnstile(...) argument of the four components and compares it with the expectedAction each handler passes (the handler tests already capture it).

### TEST-UNIT-07 [low] preorderCurrent compares a date string against the browser's en-CA locale format; its unit test proves only Node's ICU
src/components/store/VariantAction.tsx:91 fix:trivial conf:medium
EVIDENCE: `return shipsOn >= new Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-CA', { timeZone: 'Asia/Riyadh' }).format(now)` runs in the buyer's browser (a 'use client' component, used at line 151) and assumes en-CA's default date output is 'YYYY-MM-DD'. That is locale data, not a spec guarantee: CLDR 42/ICU 72 changed en-CA to M/d/y, and Chromium and Firefox later patched it back to y-MM-dd (mullvad-browser commit 32434f8e: revert the date format for en-CA per cldr PR 2759). tests/unit/store-availability.test.ts:127-136 runs under Node's bundled ICU, so it passes whatever a buyer's browser does. With '10/4/2026', `'2026-10-03' >= '10/4/2026'` is true ('2' sorts after '1'), so every past date reads as current. The codebase already computes the Riyadh day without locale data: `riyadhToday` (src/lib/admin-money.ts:389) and admin-commerce.ts:56.
IMPACT: On a browser whose ICU carries the M/d/y en-CA data, a static product page built before its preorder date passed keeps showing «طلب مسبق: يُسلَّم في …» with the past date and the preorder button, which is exactly what the guard is meant to stop. The server still decides what can be bought, so the harm is a wrong promise on the page, not a wrong order.
FIX: Compute the Riyadh day without locale data, e.g. `new Date(now.getTime() + 3 * 3_600_000).toISOString().slice(0, 10)`, or reuse riyadhToday from src/lib/admin-money.ts, and keep the existing test. Optionally add one case that stubs Intl.DateTimeFormat to return '10/4/2026', to prove the function no longer depends on it.

### TEST-UNIT-08 [low] clientKeyHash: no test would notice the pepper or the daily salt being dropped from the hashed IP
tests/unit/rate-limit.test.ts:53 fix:trivial conf:high prior:lead:TEST-UNIT-05
EVIDENCE: The only clientKeyHash test hashes two requests with the same pepper ('pepper') at the same moment and compares them (lines 55-57). rate-limit.ts:62 builds `${pepper}:${utcDate}:${throttleAddress(requestIp(request))}`. Deleting `${pepper}:` or `${utcDate}:` from that template leaves every unit test green, since no test varies the pepper or the clock. The hashes are stored as finance.rate_limits.key_hash (20260926120000_contacts_and_email.sql:49) for every throttled endpoint.
IMPACT: Without the pepper, an IPv4 hash can be reversed by hashing all 2^32 addresses, so the promise that a raw IP is never stored (rate-limit.ts:2-6, the privacy data map) silently stops holding for the rate_limits rows and every backup of them. Without the date, a visitor's hash links across days. Neither regression changes any visible behaviour, so only a test can catch it.
FIX: Add two assertions: the same request hashes differently under pepper 'a' and pepper 'b'; and, with vi.useFakeTimers({toFake:['Date']}), the same request hashes differently at 2026-10-05T23:59:59Z and 2026-10-06T00:00:01Z, and the same within one UTC day.

### TEST-UNIT-09 [low] verifyTurnstile's outgoing siteverify request is never asserted: in CI nothing pins the URL, the body fields or the remoteip rule
tests/unit/turnstile.test.ts:27 fix:trivial conf:high prior:lead:TEST-UNIT-07
EVIDENCE: reply() stubs fetch with `vi.fn(async () => new Response(...))` and no test reads fetch.mock.calls. The URL, the method and the body `{secret, response, remoteip}` built at turnstile.ts:76-85 are therefore unpinned, as are the omission of remoteip for 'local' (line 82) and the 5 s timeout (line 84). The handler tests inject `verify` and never reach this code. The only other check is the local e2e battery, which posts the dummy token to the real siteverify with the test secret (Cloudflare says test secret keys 'Only accept the dummy token, reject real tokens', developers.cloudflare.com/turnstile/troubleshooting/testing/), and CI does not run it.
IMPACT: A refactor that sends the wrong field as `response`, drops `secret`, or sends remoteip:'local' passes lint, typecheck and the unit suite in CI. It is caught only if someone runs the local e2e battery before deploying. Otherwise every form on the hosted site answers every visitor with 400 or 503: checkout, contact, recovery and sign-ups.
FIX: In the success test, keep the stub and assert one call to 'https://challenges.cloudflare.com/turnstile/v0/siteverify' with method POST and the JSON body {secret: REAL_SECRET, response: 'tok', remoteip: '203.0.113.7'}. Add a case with remoteIp 'local' that asserts the body has no remoteip key.

### TEST-UNIT-10 [low] The backup reader's own refusals (unsafe path, duplicate, manifest first, size/sha256) are never exercised; only the writer's are
tests/unit/backup-format.test.ts:324 fix:small conf:high
EVIDENCE: Every archive the tests read is made by writeBackup, which already refuses an unsafe path (test at line 324). EntryExtractor's own checks in scripts/lib/backup-format.mjs therefore never see bad input: assertSafeEntryPath(meta.path) at line 398, 'Duplicate entry path' at 403-405, 'The first entry must be manifest.json' at 399-401, the impossible header length at 354, and the size/sha256 comparison at 420-422. A grep of tests/ for those messages finds nothing. The tamper tests (lines 195-239) all fail earlier, at gunzip or at the GCM tag, as WRONG_PASSPHRASE_OR_DAMAGED. The header comment says assertSafeEntryPath is 'used by both the writer and the reader'; only its use by the writer is proven.
IMPACT: Deleting line 398, or the duplicate check, in a refactor keeps the suite green, and `pnpm restore-check --extract` would then write an entry named `../x` or `C:/...` outside the chosen directory. Exploiting it needs an archive sealed with the owner's passphrase, because GCM authenticates every byte. The realistic case is a backup file handed to the owner, not a remote attack, and the reader's checks are the last guard against exactly that case.
FIX: Add a test helper that seals a hand-built entry stream with the format's own header, gzip and AES-256-GCM (a few lines with node:crypto and node:zlib, using deriveKey). Assert that readBackup refuses with code CORRUPT and leaves nothing behind for: the entry paths '../escape.txt', 'a\\b' and 'C:/x'; a duplicate path; a first entry that is not manifest.json; a size that does not match; a wrong sha256.

### TEST-UNIT-11 [low] cart.test.ts depends on test order through the module's writeFailed and memoryCart state
tests/unit/cart.test.ts:161 fix:trivial conf:high
EVIDENCE: src/lib/cart.ts keeps `let memoryCart` (line 233) and `let writeFailed` (line 236) at module level, and readCart returns the memory cart with persistent:false whenever writeFailed is true (line 271). The test 'falls back to memory when setItem throws mid-session' (line 180) leaves writeFailed set to true. The file never resets it (no beforeEach, no vi.resetModules); the later tests pass only because each happens to begin with a successful write. 'persists through a working storage area and discards a malformed value' (line 161) starts with `expect(readCart(area)).toEqual({ cart: EMPTY_CART, persistent: true })`, which fails if it runs after line 180: it would get {cart: [VARIANT_B x1], persistent: false}. store-availability.test.ts:149-152 shows the pattern this file lacks (`vi.resetModules()` and a fresh import).
IMPACT: The suite is green only in declaration order. Running `vitest --sequence.shuffle`, filtering to a single test with .only, or moving a test makes cart tests fail for reasons unrelated to the change, and a real regression in the storage fallback can be masked or blamed on the wrong change. No product impact.
FIX: Add a beforeEach that re-imports the module after vi.resetModules(), as store-availability.test.ts does, or one that performs a successful writeCart(EMPTY_CART, memoryArea()) to clear writeFailed and memoryCart before every test.

vitest.config.ts:20 fix:trivial conf:high

A western zone also exposes date-only strings that are parsed as local time.

### VENDOR-PAY-03 [low] Resend documents three bounce types (Permanent, Transient, Undetermined); an Undetermined bounce suppresses the address locally for good while Resend itself does not, and operations.md still says Permanent/Temporary
supabase/migrations/20260930140000_audit2_fixes.sql:650 fix:trivial conf:high prior:P06 chose conservative over-suppression for undocumented types (artifacts/acceptance/P06/source-resend-bounced.md auditor note); Undetermined is now documented as not permanent. Wave A EF-ADMIN-08 covers the missing un-suppress procedure.
EVIDENCE: email_event_record suppresses every bounce outside the record-only list: `and coalesce(v_bounce_type, '') not in ('temporary', 'soft', 'transient')` (audit2_fixes.sql:650). resend.com/docs/dashboard/emails/email-bounces.md (fetched 2026-10-07): 'There are three types of bounces: Permanent ... Transient ... Undetermined - where the recipient's email server bounced, but the bounce message didn't contain enough information'; email-suppressions.md: Resend suppresses on bounce only when 'the recipient's mail server permanently rejected the email'. operations.md:318-321: 'Resend documents `bounce.type` as `Permanent`/`Temporary` ... a missing or undocumented type suppresses conservatively'.
IMPACT: One unparseable bounce (an auto-responder, an odd relay) puts a buyer or staff address in finance.email_suppressions: every later receipt, shipping, refund and file-ready mail to that buyer, or every owner alert and contact notice to that staff member, is closed 'suppressed' and cannot be replayed, while Resend would still deliver. Lifting it needs hand-written SQL (EF-ADMIN-08).
FIX: Add 'undetermined' to the record-only list in email_event_record (a real hard bounce on the next send arrives as Permanent and suppresses then) and correct operations.md's vocabulary to Permanent / Transient / Undetermined with this rule.

### VENDOR-PAY-04 [low] An email Resend accepted and then reports as email.failed (quota, API key, domain verification) is listed as «فشل» but can never be resent: outbox_replay refuses a sent row, and a same-key resend would be answered from Resend's idempotency cache

EVIDENCE: outbox_replay: `if o.status not in ('exhausted', 'uncertain') then raise exception 'Only exhausted or uncertain messages can be replayed.'` (order_emails.sql:378-379); an accepted send makes the row 'sent' and email.failed only sets delivery = 'failed' (20260930140000_audit2_fixes.sql:661-678); outbox_attention lists `o.delivery in ('bounced', 'complained', 'failed')` (order_emails.sql:422) and EmailView offers replay only for exhausted/uncertain rows (src/components/admin/EmailView.tsx:46-47). resend.com/docs/webhooks/emails/failed.md (fetched 2026-10-07): email.failed 'is triggered when there are issues such as invalid recipients, API key problems, domain verification issues, email quota limits, or other sending failures'; idempotency-keys.md: the same key within 24 hours returns 'the same response, without actually sending the email again'.
IMPACT: After a domain-verification lapse, a key problem or a quota failure Resend reports asynchronously, every affected receipt (with the buyer's order link), shipping or refund notice and owner alert stays lost: the email screen shows «فشل» with no action, operations.md gives no procedure, and DATA-AND-SECURITY's 'surface recovery in existing admin operations' is not met.
FIX: Let outbox_replay accept a 'sent' row whose delivery is 'failed' (not bounced, complained or suppressed): set it pending with a new idempotency key (the old one would replay Resend's cached answer), keep the suppression check, audit it, show the replay button for such rows, and add one line to operations.md.

### VENDOR-PLAT-02 [low] The plan treats verify_jwt = true as a gateway JWT check for admin and staff-admin, but Supabase's gateway now lets any publishable key through, so it authenticates nobody
PLANS/ARCHITECTURE.md:95 fix:trivial conf:high prior:none; wave A EF-ACCESS-14 and EF-ADMIN-03 assumed a genuine JWT was needed to reach admin
EVIDENCE: ARCHITECTURE.md:95 'Public functions set verify_jwt = false ... the rest require a Supabase JWT at the gateway and check the role again.'; supabase/config.toml:447-453 pins verify_jwt = true on admin and staff-admin 'so a future default change ... must not silently expose the staff endpoints'. Supabase (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/functions/auth-headers.md, fetched 2026-10-07): 'For migration compatibility, verify_jwt accepts publishable and secret keys on either header, so a key on apikey passes the check too. The check alone doesn't authenticate a caller that sends only an API key.' The publishable key is in every page of the export. Today both handlers verify the caller themselves before reading the body: admin.ts:184-193 (staffFromRequest, then request.text()) and staff-admin/index.ts:44-59.
IMPACT: No exposure today, because the in-function getClaims check is the real gate. The risk is the next staff-only function (P09 calendar management, P12 board move, any new admin action) written on the plan's word that the gateway already demands a staff JWT: with only verify_jwt = true and no in-code check, anyone holding the public key reaches it. Every anonymous call to admin or staff-admin is also a billed invocation (wave A EF-ACCESS-14 assumed these two were gateway-protected).
FIX: Change ARCHITECTURE.md:95 and the config.toml:447-448 comment to say the gateway check admits the public key and is not authentication; every staff function must call staffFromRequest (or @supabase/server with auth: 'user') before any other work, and the P09/P12 contracts should repeat that rule.

### VENDOR-PLAT-03 [low] secure_password_change is described as closing the password-change path, but Supabase Auth lets any session younger than 24 hours set a password without reauthentication; only the access-token hook stops password sign-in
supabase/config.toml:235 fix:trivial conf:high prior:AUDIT-1 S01.4 introduced the hook and the setting; no earlier audit checked the 24-hour rule
EVIDENCE: config.toml:234-236 '# Staff have no passwords (D08); the hook below refuses password sign-in, this closes the change path too.' / 'secure_password_change = true'; ARCHITECTURE.md:18 and DATA-AND-SECURITY.md:99 list it beside the hook as the enforcement; ISSUES.md I28 makes it a hosted step. GoTrue (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/supabase/auth/master/internal/api/user.go lines 153-165): 'if config.Security.UpdatePasswordRequireReauthentication { ... if session == nil || now.After(session.CreatedAt.Add(24*time.Hour)) { if len(params.Nonce) == 0 { ... ReauthenticationNeeded': a session created in the last 24 hours sets a password with no nonce, and an older one only needs the reauthentication code mailed to the staff inbox.
IMPACT: Any staff session (or a stolen one) can add a password to its account at once. It cannot be used to sign in while the Custom Access Token hook answers 403, so nothing is exposed today; but the hook is a hosted dashboard setting (I28), and if it is ever off or mis-wired on the hosted project the password works, while the plan believes a second layer exists.
FIX: Reword config.toml:235, ARCHITECTURE.md:18 and DATA-AND-SECURITY.md:99 to say the hook is the only control (secure_password_change only adds a reauthentication step for sessions older than 24 hours), and keep I28's password-grant 403 check as the acceptance proof of the hook on the hosted project.

### VENDOR-PLAT-05 [low] config.toml leaves auto_expose_new_tables unset and documents unset as 'not auto-exposed, removed on 2026-10-30'; Supabase CLI 2.116.0 made unset mean auto-expose and dropped that date, so any CLI upgrade silently changes the local grants
supabase/config.toml:24 fix:trivial conf:high prior:wave A DB-CORE-05 (service_role default table privileges on hosted) is the other side of the same default
EVIDENCE: config.toml:19-24: '# When unset, new entities are NOT auto-exposed, matching the new cloud default. ... deprecated and the field is removed on 2026-10-30 ...' / '# auto_expose_new_tables = true'. True for the pinned CLI 2.106.0 (release notes 2026-06-11: 'auto_expose_new_tables now resolves to false when unset'; local start/reset revoke default privileges on new public tables, sequences and functions). Supabase CLI PR #6337 (https://github.com/supabase/cli/pull/6337), merged 2026-08-26, released in v2.116.0 ('auto_expose_new_tables defaults to true'): 'The 2026-05-30 flip ... anticipating a cloud default change that never landed — platform projects still auto-expose new entities ... unset ≡ explicit true ... The deprecation warning and the 2026-10-30 removal narrative are removed'. The current CLI is v2.120.0 (2026-10-06); .github/workflows/ci.yml:82 pins 2.106.0; Supabase's changelog post 45329 still announces enforcement on all existing projects on 2026-10-30.
IMPACT: The day the CLI is bumped (CI pin or a developer's machine), `supabase start`/`db reset` stop revoking postgres's default privileges for service_role, so the local stack grants service_role full access to every new public table while the hosted project, if the 2026-10-30 enforcement lands, grants none. A later migration that forgets an explicit service_role grant then passes every local and CI test and fails on the hosted project with 42501. anon and authenticated are not affected (20260924130000 revokes their defaults itself). The comment also gives an operator a removal date the CLI has withdrawn.
FIX: Set `auto_expose_new_tables = false` explicitly under [api] in supabase/config.toml (the behaviour every current test already runs on) and rewrite the comment to say the platform default and its date are contested; add to I28 a hosted check of `pg_default_acl` for schema public after the migrations.

### VENDOR-PLAT-07 [low] next 16.3.5 is inside a critical advisory's range (GHSA-vcvr-r3jv-pc5j, next/og ImageResponse RCE, fixed in 16.3.6); not reachable here, but P10's 'no silently suppressed critical' gate will stop on it
package.json:40 fix:trivial conf:high prior:same wave: SUPPLY-03 (pnpm audit --prod fails on next 16.3.5); no earlier audit predates the advisory (2026-09-30)
EVIDENCE: package.json:40 `"next": "16.3.5"` (eslint-config-next 16.3.5 at line 55). GitHub Advisory Database (https://api.github.com/advisories?ecosystem=npm&affects=next, fetched 2026-10-07): GHSA-vcvr-r3jv-pc5j, published 2026-09-30, critical, 'Remote Code Execution in next/og ImageResponse', vulnerable '>= 16.2.0, < 16.3.6'. It needs attacker-controlled values in the Node ImageResponse's SVG; `grep -rn 'next/og|ImageResponse|opengraph-image' src` finds nothing and a static export renders at build time only, so the site is not exploitable. npm has 16.3.6-16.3.8 and 16.4.0 since. PLANS/VERIFICATION.md:57: 'No silently suppressed critical/high runtime vulnerability.' pdfjs-dist 6.3.289 is outside GHSA-hq66-cqwq-w95j (fixed 6.2.108); @supabase/supabase-js 2.117.1 has no advisory.
IMPACT: No exposure today. The P10 dependency gate (pnpm audit) reports a critical on the main framework and must upgrade or record an exception; and if P10's per-route OG work generates social cards with next/og from published titles, an editor-controlled string would reach the vulnerable renderer at build time.
FIX: Move next and eslint-config-next to 16.3.8 (patch releases only) before P10's metadata work, with the usual check/build/export battery; require >= 16.3.6 if next/og is ever added.

### VENDOR-PLAT-08 [low] The P10 CSP specification lists script-src for Turnstile but not the frame-src Turnstile requires, and no img-src for the Supabase Storage host that serves every library image
PLANS/DATA-AND-SECURITY.md:101 fix:trivial conf:high prior:same wave: ARCH-06 (the same CSP omissions); I46 assigns the full CSP to P10; wave A CLIENT-SEC-05 covers HSTS/COOP
EVIDENCE: DATA-AND-SECURITY.md:101 defines P10's full CSP as `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`, `script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' https://challenges.cloudflare.com` without unsafe-eval, `worker-src 'self'`, `connect-src` for the Supabase Data API and /functions/v1, and the Web Analytics hosts if enabled; VERIFICATION.md:45 repeats it as the P10 gate. Cloudflare (https://developers.cloudflare.com/turnstile/reference/content-security-policy/, May 5, 2026): 'you must configure it to allow Turnstile's scripts and iframes ... script-src: https://challenges.cloudflare.com / frame-src: https://challenges.cloudflare.com'. Library images come from `${NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL}/storage/v1/object/public/media-public` (src/lib/media-ref.ts:15-16), another origin. Local runs cannot catch either: next dev does not apply public/_headers, and several specs stub the Turnstile script (docs/development.md:369-376).
IMPACT: If P10 writes the listed directives under a restrictive default-src (the usual base), the Turnstile iframe is blocked on /contact, the availability sign-up, order-link recovery and checkout, so every public form fails closed, and every library image (journal covers, product photos) is blocked. Only the hosted-preview check would show it, after the headers ship.
FIX: Amend the CSP list in DATA-AND-SECURITY.md:101 and VERIFICATION.md:45 with `frame-src https://challenges.cloudflare.com`, `img-src 'self' data: <the Supabase project origin>` (plus `media-src` for the films' host if I33 moves them), `font-src 'self'` and `connect-src 'self'` beside the Supabase origins; keep the hosted-preview check.

### ARCH-11 [info] Observation: the product and store pages show the build's price while the cart and checkout show and charge the live one; nothing tells the buyer when they differ
src/app/(public)/store/[slug]/page.tsx:104 fix:small conf:high
EVIDENCE: The product page passes `price={formatMoney(variant.priceHalalas)}` from the build-time catalog (store/[slug]/page.tsx:104; store/page.tsx:63 shows «من …» the same way); CartView says 'prices come from the live `quote` only' (src/components/store/CartView.tsx:4) and checkout_create re-prices under the row locks and refuses a changed quote with QUOTE_CHANGED (DATA-AND-SECURITY:34). The variant trigger requests a rebuild on a price change (20261002100000_payment_core.sql:74-87), coalesced to at most one build per two minutes.
IMPACT: A buyer is always charged the price the checkout showed, so no money is wrong. Between a price change and the next successful build (minutes, or indefinitely while builds fail: ARCH-01) the product page advertises a different price from the cart's, with no sentence saying the price changed.
FIX: None required; optionally have catalog_availability also return the current price so VariantAction can show it (or hide the built price) when it differs.

### TEST-UNIT-12 [info] The test:db guard accepts any loopback Postgres, so three integration files would run against another project's local stack on the same port
vitest.config.ts:15 fix:small conf:medium
EVIDENCE: `if (mode === 'db') assertLocalTestDatabase()` checks only that TEST_ENV is 'local' and that the DATABASE_URL host is loopback (src/lib/env.ts:14-29). tests/integration/support.ts:26-34 additionally requires this project's `supabase status`, but buyer-retention.test.ts:13-15, cron-jobs.test.ts:14-16 and static-site.test.ts:12-14 do not import support.ts. They connect to `process.env.DATABASE_URL ?? 'postgresql://postgres:postgres@127.0.0.1:54322/postgres'`. Port 54322 is the default every local Supabase project uses, and the brief for this audit records that 54321-54324 currently belong to another project's stack.
IMPACT: If `pnpm test:db` is started while another project's stack holds the ports, the files that use support.ts fail at import, but those three connect to the other project's database as `postgres`. Today they only read the catalog or write inside transactions they roll back, so the result is confusing failures, not damage. A future standalone file that commits would write into the other project's local data, which the guard's comment ('a disposable local PostgreSQL instance') says cannot happen.
FIX: In assertLocalTestDatabase, or in a shared helper the three files use, also require a marker that only this project's schema has, e.g. `select to_regprocedure('finance.buyer_retention_purge()') is not null`. Alternatively, derive the URL from `supabase status -o json` (DB_URL), as support.ts does for the API.

### TEST-UNIT-14 [info] 'compares each bucket with the restored database's own rows' only greps the script's source text
tests/unit/restore-check.test.ts:43 fix:small conf:high
EVIDENCE: The test reads scripts/restore-check.mjs as text and asserts `toMatch(/group by bucket_id/)` and `toMatch(/object rows in the restored database/)`. The comparison it names is at restore-check.mjs:457-464: the loop `for (const bucket of new Set([...BUCKETS, ...objects.perBucket.keys(), ...bucketRows.keys()]))` and its ok/FAIL decision for each bucket. Any change to that logic that keeps the SQL string and the message (an inverted condition, a bucket dropped from the set) still passes. The test at line 37 does the same for `file_size_limit`.
IMPACT: The test name tells a reader the per-bucket check is proven, but it is not. The real proof is the owner's restore rehearsal (D35), which needs Docker and is run rarely, so a broken check could let a backup without the paid files pass `pnpm restore-check`.
FIX: Move the per-bucket decision into a pure exported function (inputs: BUCKETS, the verified and total counts per bucket, the restored row counts; output: the list of problems). Unit-test it with a missing paid-files bucket, an empty bucket and a count mismatch, and keep the source grep only if it still adds something.

### VENDOR-PAY-09 [info] The Svix verifier is tested only against the test file's own re-implementation of the signing scheme; it does match Svix's published vector, but no test pins that
tests/unit/resend-webhook.test.ts:51 fix:trivial conf:high prior:none
EVIDENCE: The test signs with `createHmac('sha256', key).update(`${svixId}.${timestamp}.${rawBody}`).digest('base64')` (resend-webhook.test.ts:46-51), the same reading of the scheme the verifier implements (email.ts:651-665), so a shared misreading would pass both. A read-only scratch script ran the repository's verifySvixSignature against Svix's published example (https://docs.svix.com/receiving/verifying-payloads/how-manual: secret whsec_plJ3nmyCDGBKInavdOK15jsl, msg_loFOjxBNrRLzqYUf, timestamp 1731705121, signature v1,rAvfW3dJ/X/qxhsaXPOyyCGmRKsaKWcsNccKXlIktD0=): accepted; a changed body and a timestamp 6 minutes old were refused.
IMPACT: None today. A later change to the verifier or to the helper could drift from Svix's real scheme without a test failing, and every Resend event (bounces, complaints) would then be refused with 401 until Resend disables the endpoint.
FIX: Add one unit case with Svix's published vector (secret, id, timestamp, body, signature as on the page; now fixed to the timestamp) expecting true.

# Plan gaps
- [TEST-UNIT] before_launch | P10 | Run the database suite (or its SQL-only files) in CI
  WHY: CI runs `pnpm test` only (.github/workflows/ci.yml, closing comment). Every unit test of an Edge Function scripts the SQL replies (payments, checkout, download, notify, orders, admin), and their headers point to tests/integration for the real invariants: deny-by-default RLS, finance isolation, the owner and TOTP gates with real JWTs, apply_verified_payment's amount, currency and mode checks, the refund balance, and idempotency. Those run only when someone runs `pnpm test:db` locally, so a regression in a migration or in an authorization line merges green (see TEST-UNIT-03, -05 and -06). The CI job already starts Postgres, PostgREST, Auth and Storage for the build, so the SQL-only files (no edge runtime, no Mailpit) could run there at small cost.
  TOUCHES: .github/workflows/ci.yml; vitest.config.ts (an include list or tag for SQL-only db tests); the tests/integration files that need no edge runtime or Mailpit
- [TEST-E2E] before_P09 | P10 | Run the database suite in CI, first without and then with the edge runtime
  WHY: No security, RLS, grant or money invariant is checked on push (TEST-E2E-11). Smallest honest step, no new service, about +3-6 min: in job verify, after check:budgets (so test data cannot reach the export), `TEST_ENV=local DATABASE_URL=postgresql://postgres:postgres@127.0.0.1:54322/postgres pnpm exec vitest run --mode db --exclude "tests/integration/{staff-admin,checkout-http,disputes-http,notify-http,orders-http,payment-http,refunds-http,order-emails}.test.ts"` (22 files; the in-process emulator binds 54390 on the runner). Full step, about +8-15 min, timeout-minutes 30 -> 45: drop edge-runtime and mailpit from `supabase start -x`; after db:env run `supabase stop` then `supabase start -x ...` again, because the local stack loads supabase/functions/.env on start (supabase.com/docs/guides/functions/secrets); then `pnpm test:db`. The HTTP files start their own emulator on 54390, reached by the edge-runtime container at host.docker.internal (scripts/local-env.mjs:51-54): verify that the CLI 2.106.0 maps it on Linux runners, else write the Docker bridge gateway for CI. Keep e2e local or nightly (orders.spec alone takes 7-11 min). Estimates are from the job's current shape and the recorded suite sizes (397 db tests, 20 files at P08 round 4b, now 30), not measured.
  TOUCHES: .github/workflows/ci.yml (new steps, comment, timeout); possibly scripts/local-env.mjs for the container-to-host address in CI
- [TEST-E2E] before_launch | P11 | Bind the hosted site to a CI-green commit and prove which commit is live (I40 deploy gate)
  WHY: Nothing in the repository configures the Pages build or a deploy; Cloudflare deploy hooks build the configured branch on every POST with no status-check option (developers.cloudflare.com/pages/configuration/deploy-hooks), and site_build_trigger fires one on every publish, so a commit that failed CI on the production branch ships at the next publish. The export records no commit, so no check can tell which code is live.
  TOUCHES: Pages project settings (production branch fast-forwarded only by a CI job after green; build command `pnpm build && pnpm check:export`), out/version.txt written from CF_PAGES_COMMIT_SHA (developers.cloudflare.com/pages/configuration/build-configuration), a post-deploy check comparing https://anas.studio/version.txt with the green SHA

The plan has none of these (PLAN-GAPS-11, -12); Anas asked for drafts (Q42, Q43); refund timing to state (Moyasar FAQ: mada 24 hours to 3 business days, credit cards 7 to 14 business days).

- [FIX-A2] before_checkout_opens | P08 | I48 (1)'s approval race (GAP-G5-3) is not closed by P08's built-revision design
  WHY: P08 closed the static-build half of I48 (1): the checkout sends the revisions the build rendered and waits while they differ from the approved ones (CheckoutForm.tsx:205,313-317; cart.ts builtPolicyRevisions). The race AUDIT-2 GAP-G5-3 named is still in the SQL: commerce_policies_approve (last defined 20260930140000_audit2_fixes.sql:83-141) locks only the settings row and reads published_documents.seq with no lock, and policies_reset_approval (20260930120000_audit_fixes.sql:141-145) resets only when the key is already in policy_revisions, so its UPDATE matches no row and takes no lock when the approval was empty. A policy publish whose transaction overlaps a first approval can leave the approval on the older seq; today that fails closed (every checkout waits with «نحدّث السياسات الآن؛ حاول بعد قليل.» until the owner approves again) and the settings screen lists only approved revisions (CommerceSettingsForm.tsx:170-176), not the live ones. When the plan documents close I48 at P08's end, (1) must not be closed on the built-revision change alone. (The race itself is also reported as findings by wave A DB-CORE-03 and DB-COMMERCE-05; this entry is about the I48 record.)
  TOUCHES: a new forward migration (lock the settings row unconditionally in policies_reset_approval, or read the policies' published rows FOR SHARE in commerce_policies_approve); src/components/admin/CommerceSettingsForm.tsx (approved vs live seq); PLANS/ISSUES.md I48

The reason given in ci.yml:111-114 (needs the whole stack and a browser) does not hold for test:db: it is Vitest, Supabase documents `supabase start` plus tests on GitHub runners (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/deployment/ci/testing), and private-repo runners have 2 CPU, 8 GB RAM, 14 GB SSD.

Under VERIFICATION.md:59's RTO<=4h the owner would be writing a script during an outage.

- [ARCH] before_launch | P11 | Pages Git settings and release order: preview builds for agent branches off, and production deploys only after CI and after the migrations and functions they need
  WHY: Cloudflare Pages by default "will automatically deploy any and every commit to a preview branch" and every push to the production branch (https://developers.cloudflare.com/pages/configuration/branch-build-controls/). The repository practice is many commits on agent/ branches merged into main (148 commits on main since 2026-09-07). Connected as P11 describes, every agent push becomes a public preview build against the production Supabase project, every build (preview included) counts against the 500 a month that content publishes need, and every push to main deploys to anas.studio without waiting for CI. The three deploy units are released separately: Pages builds on push, while migrations (supabase db push over DATABASE_URL, D26) and Edge Functions (supabase functions deploy) are manual CLI steps, so a push whose pages call a new RPC, column grant or function action is live before what it needs exists (the alt_ar grant note in docs/operations.md:415-419 is one instance). There is one live project and no staging (DECISIONS: one free live Supabase project).
  TOUCHES: PLANS/WORK-PACKAGES.md P11 hosting line; docs/deployment.md and docs/rollback.md (P11); .github/workflows/ci.yml if CI is to call the deploy hook after tests and migrations; Pages Settings > Builds (production auto-deploy, preview branch control)
- [ARCH] before_launch | P11 | Rollback for a static site whose artifact carries the data: Pages rollback restores old content and the next publish silently undoes it
  WHY: A Pages rollback switches production to an earlier deployment, which holds that build's data as well as its code (archived posts back, superseded policy seqs that the checkout then sends, old prices). The next publish, scheduled post or catalog change calls the deploy hook, which builds the branch head again, so the rolled-back bad code returns with no one noticing. docs/rollback.md (P11) must say: undo code by reverting the commit on main and letting the hook rebuild; use a Pages rollback only as a stopgap and expect the next publish to replace it; never roll the database back with the static site. The database side has no down path at all: supabase/migrations is forward-only, so undoing a schema change is a new forward migration or a restore (D35), and a restore into a new project also needs the site repointed (ARCH-09). The three units (migrations, functions, Pages) therefore roll back differently, and the runbook has to say in which order.
  TOUCHES: docs/rollback.md, docs/deployment.md (P11); PLANS/WORK-PACKAGES.md P11
- [ARCH] before_launch OWNER | P10 | Pushed alerts for the failures a static site hides: the one external monitor watches a page that cannot fail with the backend
  WHY: D23/ARCHITECTURE:113 put the single UptimeRobot check on the home page, a static file on Cloudflare's CDN that keeps answering 200 through every failure this architecture can have: a paused or restricted Supabase project (ARCH-02), failing Pages builds (ARCH-01), a stopped pg_cron or pg_net or a failing pure-SQL job (ARCH-13), a wrong Vault value or JOBS_SECRET (outbox_kick and payments_kick never read their HTTP answers), Edge Function errors before Sentry exists. Every other signal is on the owner home, which nobody sees unless they sign in; the owner alert mails themselves depend on the outbox, the cron and Resend. Cheap pushed signals exist without new providers: Cloudflare's free Pages 'Deployment failed' notification, Supabase's pause and quota mails to the account owner, and the same UptimeRobot check pointed at a public endpoint that answers 200 only when the newest job runs are fresh (or a keyword check on a page whose content needs the backend).
  TOUCHES: PLANS/DECISIONS.md D23 (monitor target), PLANS/WORK-PACKAGES.md P10 (monitor) and P11 (alerts to the owner), docs/monitoring.md (P10)
- [ARCH] before_launch | P11 | A hosted first-owner bootstrap: nothing can create the first staff row on the hosted project
  WHY: scripts/bootstrap-owner.mjs refuses any non-local API URL (lines 11, 24-27), sign-up is off, staff-admin and every invite need an existing owner at aal2, and pnpm db:import is loopback-only; I41 says the hosted content arrives 'through the P11 content bootstrap or the owner in the admin', but no plan line, ISSUES item or runbook says how the first owner (Auth user plus staff row) is created on the hosted project, and the E02 sandbox proof on the hosted project needs that owner first.
  TOUCHES: scripts/bootstrap-owner.mjs (an explicit, confirmed hosted mode) or a documented SQL-editor procedure; PLANS/WORK-PACKAGES.md P11; docs/deployment.md; PLANS/ISSUES.md I41
- [ARCH] before_P10 | P10 | P10 Sentry in the Deno functions: per-request scopes and a flush, or PII and breadcrumbs cross requests and events are lost
  WHY: Supabase's own guide (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/functions/examples/sentry-monitoring) says the Sentry Deno SDK 'currently do[es] not support Deno.serve instrumentation, which means that there is no scope separation between requests', so it disables default integrations and wraps every call in withScope, and it awaits Sentry.flush(2000) before returning. The functions reuse an isolate across requests (db.ts caches its client per isolate), handle buyer emails, addresses, tokens and webhook bodies, and D23 requires PII to be stripped; P10's file list only says 'Sentry for the Edge Functions under _shared/'. Without these rules one buyer's context lands on another request's event, and an error answered before the flush is never sent.
  TOUCHES: supabase/functions/_shared (a sentry helper used by every entrypoint), PLANS/WORK-PACKAGES.md P10, docs/monitoring.md
- [ARCH] before_P09 | P09 | P09 slot holds need their own unpaid-hold rule: the per-recipient limit P09 is told to reuse no longer exists
  WHY: WORK-PACKAGES P09 says 'reuse P07's hold protections for paid slots' and DATA-AND-SECURITY:69 still promises 'active-hold limits by guest checkout session and normalized recipient/resource', but P08-CONTRACT section 13.1 removed the per-email hold (what remains: one hold per checkout session, 10 creates an hour per IP hash, Turnstile, 500 orders a day; docs/operations.md residuals). P08-CONTRACT is not among the documents every package includes by reference (WORK-PACKAGES.md:3). A booking slot is a single unit, so one fresh session per slot (Turnstile passes, a few IPs) can keep every paid slot of the week held for 20 minutes at a time, round the clock; the exclusion constraint guarantees no double booking, not availability. Wave A's DB-COMMERCE plan gap asks for a per-resource limit for stock; slots need the decision before P09 dispatch, and DATA-AND-SECURITY needs P08's section 13 folded in.
  TOUCHES: PLANS/DATA-AND-SECURITY.md (Unpaid reservation abuse; fold in P08-CONTRACT section 13), PLANS/WORK-PACKAGES.md P09, the P09 booking hold function
- [ARCH] before_launch | P11 | Hosted check of the deploy hook's answers while a build is running or queued, before the runbook's 'five failures mean the hook is broken' is trusted
  WHY: site_build_trigger counts any non-2xx answer as a failed call and re-arms it up to five times (20260930140000_audit2_fixes.sql:737-759); docs/operations.md:40-41 tells the owner that «فاشل» after five tries 'means the hook itself is broken'. Cloudflare documents no answer codes for deploy hooks (https://developers.cloudflare.com/pages/configuration/deploy-hooks/) and changed queueing on 2026-08-11 (superseded queued builds are skipped); a third-party report from 2026-08-05 saw a hook answer HTTP 304 while builds for the branch were queued behind a stuck one (https://flaviocopes.com/fix-stuck-cloudflare-pages-build-queue/). If that still happens, a stuck or long queue shows as a broken hook and sends the owner to regenerate it instead of cancelling the stuck build.
  TOUCHES: I32 (hosted checks), docs/operations.md 'Site rebuilds (I34)', possibly site_build_trigger's status test

  WHY: No document covers any of these (OPS-PRIVACY-03, -04, -05, -09, -12); P11's file list (WORK-PACKAGES.md:139: deployment, owner-guide, account-inventory, launch-checklist, training-record, support-log, rollback, license-register) has no incident or security runbook, and Vault values cannot be changed with the one command the docs give.

  WHY: E08 requires a processor/location review and a tested request procedure, but docs/privacy-data-map.md has none of these elements and omits the media library, the seller's own data and provider-side IP logging (OPS-PRIVACY-08, -15); nothing in PLANS mentions PDPL, a Supabase region or breach handling.

  WHY: E02's closure evidence names live payments (DECISIONS.md E02), the runbook's runs are all sandbox (OPS-PRIVACY-16), and P11 states 'No live purchases are authorized by this plan' (WORK-PACKAGES.md:145), so as written the first live payment is a customer's.

- [OPS-PRIVACY] before_launch OWNER | P09 or a P08 follow-up round | Retention for sent mail rows and for backup files
  WHY: No job purges sent outbox rows (the P06 comment deferring it never landed), so every recipient address stays for ever, including sign-ups that were purged (OPS-PRIVACY-07); backups have no age-out although the plan says they have (OPS-PRIVACY-15). Both periods are E08's to set; the purge itself is a small job.

- [VENDOR-PAY] before_launch OWNER | P08 (E02 runs) / P11 go-live | Decide and record the live account's payment methods: SADAD off unless manual refunds are accepted, and a manual 'refunded outside Moyasar' record if it stays
  WHY: SADAD appears on the hosted invoice page when enabled (on by default in test), leaves the invoice on_hold and cannot be refunded or voided through the API (docs.moyasar.com/docs/guides/sadad-bill/basic-integration.md). The plan has no step that chooses the methods and no way to record an out-of-band refund (VENDOR-PAY-01).

  WHY: The create-invoice reference's url example ends with ?lang=en and nothing documents an Arabic option; the store is Arabic only and hands the buyer the url as given. E02 run 1 does not record the url's query or the page language.

  WHY: Moyasar applies merchant-arranged BIN coupons automatically and lowers the payment amount, putting #coupon_* keys in metadata (docs.moyasar.com/docs/guides/coupons/index.md); apply_verified_payment would file every discounted payment as an AMOUNT_MISMATCH review payment that is never fulfilled.
  TOUCHES: docs/payments-runbook.md (a rule: arrange no coupon with Moyasar), or apply_verified_payment reading the coupon metadata
- [VENDOR-PLAT] before_P11 | P11 | Record the Cloudflare Pages versus Workers Static Assets decision before P11 builds the hosting
  WHY: D32 and P11 put the static export on a new Cloudflare Pages project. Cloudflare now says 'Workers Static Assets is the recommended way to deploy static sites ... If you are starting a new project, use Workers instead of Pages. Pages continues to work, but new features and optimizations are focused on Workers' (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/best-practices/workers-best-practices/; migration guide updated Sep 22, 2026). The choice changes P11: the SITE-STATE gate is native on Workers (run_worker_first patterns on the same Worker, instead of a Worker route or a Pages _middleware whose responses _headers do not reach); the soon Worker anas-studio-soon already is a Workers Static Assets project on the domain, so no custom-domain move (a Worker Custom Domain cannot coexist with the CNAME a Pages custom domain creates, and Pages warns of errors until a re-pointed domain is active); Workers Builds deploy hooks (since 2026-04-01) deduplicate bursts and are limited to 10/min, while every Pages deploy-hook POST builds against 500 builds a month; Workers Builds Free is metered in build minutes (3,000/month). Either way the hook URL in Vault, _headers, 404 handling (explicit not_found_handling on Workers) and film range support (VENDOR-PLAT-04) must be re-checked on the chosen platform.
  TOUCHES: PLANS/DECISIONS.md (a D-entry amending D32's hosting line), PLANS/WORK-PACKAGES.md P11, PLANS/SITE-STATE-CONTRACT.md 'What P11 decides', docs/costs.md, artifacts/soon/{wrangler.toml,README.md}
- [VENDOR-PLAT] before_P11 | P11 | Add a hosted check that verify_jwt = true on admin and staff-admin accepts the project's current JWT signing key
  WHY: Supabase's docs disagree: https://supabase.com/docs/guides/functions/auth-headers.md says the platform check validates HS256 and asymmetric signing-key JWTs, while https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/signing-keys.md says rotating to the new signing keys 'might break your app' for functions with Verify JWT and 'You will need to turn off this setting'. A gateway 401 on admin and staff-admin would stop media uploads, stats, refunds, disputes, commerce settings and the team screen. Neither I28 nor I32 lists it.
  TOUCHES: PLANS/ISSUES.md I28 or I32; supabase/config.toml:447-453 if it fails (verify_jwt = false is safe there because both handlers verify the caller first, VENDOR-PLAT-02)
- [VENDOR-PLAT] before_launch OWNER | P11 | Say what a Free-plan pause does to the launched site, and either start on Pro or give the owner the resume procedure
  WHY: D34 lets the site launch before payments and DECISIONS leaves 'whether the site launches on Free before orders' to the owner, but ARCHITECTURE.md:55 calls the pause 'harmless before orders' and no runbook mentions it. A Free project with low activity over 7 days is paused after a warning email (https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/free-project-pausing.md); the static pages stay up, but every library image (served from the project's Storage host), the contact form, availability sign-ups, the admin and staff sign-in, scheduled publishing and rebuilds stop until someone presses Resume; the SITE-STATE gate falls back to MODE. Static page views do not query the database, so a quiet week can trigger it. Free also keeps Auth logs 1 hour and platform logs 1 day (https://supabase.com/pricing). Same wave: ARCH-02, OPS-PRIVACY-09 and the PLAN-GAPS slice raise the same question.
  TOUCHES: PLANS/ARCHITECTURE.md:55, docs/operations.md ('The owner's routine'), PLANS/DECISIONS.md (Free-or-Pro launch choice), docs/costs.md

# Vendor claims false or unverifiable
- [TEST-UNIT] unverifiable: With a Turnstile test secret, siteverify answers success with hostname 'example.com' and no action field, so both checks are skipped (supabase/functions/_shared/turnstile.ts:26-41; tests/unit/turnstile.test.ts:125-129) — The current page https://developers.cloudflare.com/turnstile/troubleshooting/testing/ (fetched 2026-10-07) shows an example success with hostname 'localhost' and action 'test', and says test secrets 'Only accept the dummy token, reject real tokens'. The live answer recorded in artifacts/acceptance/P06/siteverify-live-test-secret.json has hostname example.com and no action. The code skips both checks for a test secret, so either answer passes; it was not re-tested live (this audit makes no third-party calls).
- [TEST-E2E] unverifiable: The always-pass test secret accepts ANY token, so a 400 TURNSTILE cannot be produced locally (tests/e2e/owner-operations.spec.ts:308-311) — Cloudflare's page now says "Test secret keys: Only accept the dummy token, reject real tokens" (testing/index.md line 117); the repo probe artifacts/acceptance/P06/siteverify-live-non-dummy-token.json (2026-09-26) shows success:true for a non-dummy token. Settling it needs a live siteverify call, not made here.
- [TEST-E2E] false: Playwright's toBeVisible proves an element can be seen (tests/e2e/no-js.spec.ts, public.spec.ts, visual.spec.ts (used as proof of readable content without JS)) — https://playwright.dev/docs/actionability: "Element is considered visible when it has non-empty bounding box and does not have visibility:hidden computed style ... Elements with opacity:0 are considered visible." Measured: clip-path inset(100%) also reports visible (scratch measure/visible-hidden.cjs).
- [PLAN-GAPS] false: The functions can keep using the legacy service-role key Supabase injects (SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY) for the hosted launch (PLANS/ARCHITECTURE.md:109 ('The service-role key is provided to the functions by Supabase and is never set by hand'); supabase/functions/_shared/db.ts:30) — https://supabase.com/docs/guides/api/api-keys (read 2026-10-07): 'Supabase is deprecating the `anon` and `service_role` keys by the end of 2026.' Secret keys are injected by the runtime instead. The code side is wave-A EF-ADMIN-01; no plan package schedules the switch.
- [PLAN-GAPS] false: The site runs no Pages Functions, so no Workers usage is billed; no Workers plan is needed (docs/costs.md:11; PLANS/DECISIONS.md:79, 87) — True of today's static site, false for the launch configuration D47 plans (a Worker route or Pages Function in front of the site): 'Requests to Pages functions count towards your quota for Workers plans' (https://developers.cloudflare.com/pages/platform/limits/); Workers Free '100,000 requests/day', then fail open or Error 1027 (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/platform/limits/). PLAN-GAPS-06.
- [PLAN-GAPS] false: Sentry on the Edge Functions reports checkout, webhook and scheduler errors with `beforeSend` scrubbing as the PII control (PLANS/DECISIONS.md:31 (D23); PLANS/research-final.md:62; PLANS/WORK-PACKAGES.md:127) — https://supabase.com/docs/guides/functions/examples/sentry-monitoring: `npm:@sentry/deno`; 'Sentry Deno SDK currently do not support `Deno.serve` instrumentation' and 'there is no scope separation between requests', so breadcrumbs and context are shared across concurrent requests; it advises disabling default integrations and using `withScope`, and awaits `Sentry.flush(2000)` before answering.
- [PLAN-GAPS] unverifiable: Apple Pay on the hosted invoice page needs nothing from the site's own domain (docs/payments-runbook.md:331 and run 7) — https://docs.moyasar.com/docs/guides/apple-pay/web-registration.md registers 'the correct hostname that your Apple Pay payment will start from' for web implementations; no page read states the rule for invoices hosted by Moyasar. The runbook rightly leaves it to sandbox run 7.
- [PLAN-GAPS] unverifiable: verify_jwt = true on `admin` and `staff-admin` keeps working after the hosted project moves to the new JWT signing keys (supabase/config.toml ([functions.admin], [functions.staff-admin])) — Supabase pages disagree: https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/signing-keys 'If you're using Edge Functions that have the Verify JWT setting, continuing with the rotation might break your app. You will need to turn off this setting.' versus the functions auth-headers page: 'The `verify_jwt` platform check validates legacy HS256 JWTs and JWTs signed with the new asymmetric signing keys.'
- [PLAN-GAPS] unverifiable: Receipts showing the seller's name and the amount paid, not presented as tax invoices, are what the buyer needs (no tax, D34) (PLANS/DECISIONS.md:64; supabase/functions/_shared/email.ts:377-430) — ZATCA e-invoicing applies to VAT taxpayers (https://zatca.gov.sa/en/E-Invoicing/Introduction/Pages/What-is-e-invoicing.aspx), so no tax invoice is due before registration (SAR 375,000 mandatory threshold per secondary sources). The E-Commerce Law's article 8 separately asks for an invoice with each item's cost, the total with fees and delivery, and the delivery date and place (https://laws.boe.gov.sa/BoeLaws/Laws/LawDetails/360de590-0286-4fa5-a243-aa9100c31979/1); the receipt lacks the delivery address and date. Owner must confirm.
- [PLAN-GAPS] false: A lost authenticator has a recovery path besides the owner's own device (not stated; src/components/admin/MfaEnroll.tsx:42-46 keeps one verified factor and offers no add or replace) — https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/auth-mfa mentions a user who 'has lost their authenticator device' but documents no recovery codes; recovery needs someone with dashboard or service-role access to remove the factor, which no runbook describes.
- [FIX-A2] unverifiable: A Resend send refused with a daily-quota 429 started no idempotency window for its key, so the retry may reuse the key with a re-rendered body (the premise of AUDIT-2 DB-auditfix-3's QUOTA give-back, which clears first_attempt_at and keeps the key) (supabase/migrations/20260930140000_audit2_fixes.sql:507-513 and :569-576) — Resend's idempotency page (https://resend.com/docs/dashboard/emails/idempotency-keys) says only that keys are kept 24 hours and that a key reused with a different payload answers 409 invalid_idempotent_request; it does not say whether a 429-refused request stores the key. P08 R5-05 records that live-rendered kinds can differ between attempts; if Resend stores the key on a 429, a receipt re-rendered after a quota day would end IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT. Worth one sandbox question with the real key.

# Coverage
## TEST-UNIT
The slice is tests/unit (68 files), the code each exercises, vitest.config.ts, the unit claims in PLANS/VERIFICATION.md and the acceptance commands.txt files (P00-P07, AUDIT-1/2, CLEANUP-1, DESIGN-B), and the baseline artifacts/acceptance/FABLE-AUDIT/before/test.log.

READ IN FULL, test and the source it exercises: vitest.config.ts, package.json, ci.yml, tokens, env (+src/lib/env.ts, _shared/env.ts), rate-limit, turnstile, recent-totp (+staff.ts), http, format, money, money-input, money-field-invalid, money-field-remount, resend-webhook (+email.ts verifySvixSignature), passphrase, backup-format (+scripts/lib/backup-format.mjs), restore-check (+restore-check.mjs head and bucket compare), backup-cli, reader-mapping (+book-preview.ts), payments (+payments.ts), moyasar-client (+payments/moyasar.ts), checkout (+checkout.ts), cart (+cart.ts), download (+download.ts), notify (+notify.ts), orders (+orders.ts), email (+email.ts), outbox (+outbox.ts), contact-route (+contact.ts), contact-prefill, admin-function (+admin.ts routing/stats/status, jobs.ts), admin-payments, staff-admin-function (+staff-admin/index.ts), service-client-errors, table-rules, demo-guard (+demo-guard.mjs), media (+media.ts), richtext, richtext-editor, cron-jobs (+cron-jobs.mjs), images, book-keys, pdf-open, prepare-media-manifest, quote, site-pages, admin-publish, content, content-loaders, journal, media-folders, design-b.

READ IN PART (structure, every test name, key bodies): admin-refunds (who may, replay, request hash), admin-money (refund body, key life), admin-disputes (validation, Riyadh day), admin-orders, admin-paid-files, admin-commerce, orders-page, store-checkout, store-availability, store, analytics, collections (fixtures and grants), admin-editor, admin-ops, media-library, media-ref (+media-ref.ts), book-room. moyasar-emulator.test.ts: test names and the server, fault and guard tests, compared against artifacts/acceptance/P08/moyasar-sandbox-2026-10-02.md and moyasar-docs-2026-10-02.md.

MEASURED (read-only, scripts in SCRATCH): node startup and --help times of restore-check and backup on this machine; pdf.js import and parse of the preview; formatDate with and without its zone, under Riyadh and UTC.

WHAT THE STUBBED-RPC HANDLER TESTS PROVE, AND WHAT THEY DO NOT: the Edge Function tests (payments, checkout, download, notify, orders, admin-*) prove the gates (origin on all six public functions, method, content type, size, schema), the exact rpc arguments, the mapping of SQL codes to HTTP statuses, which provider calls are made, and that no token, secret or body leaks. They do not prove SQL behaviour, authorization inside SQL, idempotency or concurrency; every file header says those are proven in tests/integration, which CI does not run (plan gap). The exceptions where the mock removes the gate itself are TEST-UNIT-05 and -06.

EMULATOR: paid payments, refunds, partial refunds, webhooks and callbacks are proven only against the emulator's own model. The sandbox record lists these as open E02 items (3, 6-11), and the emulator models the undocumented alternatives with switches (refuseSecondRefund, partialRefundKeepsPaid, cancelPaidReturns200), which unit tests drive through stubs. Not a new finding.

TIME DEPENDENCE CHECKED: tomorrow() in admin-disputes and ago() in the notify it.each tables are computed when the test file loads; they would fail only if Riyadh midnight fell, or more than 60 s passed, between loading and running, which is not worth a finding. No test found that fails in Riyadh but passes in UTC; the reverse masking is TEST-UNIT-15. No skip, todo or only markers except content.test.ts:26, whose skip of the drift check in CI was already ruled on as AUDIT-2 X-HEALTH-1. No unawaited resolves/rejects, no catch that swallows an assertion, no expect without a matcher.

LEADS: 01 and 02 confirmed and merged into TEST-UNIT-01; 05 is TEST-UNIT-08; 06 is TEST-UNIT-06 (raised to medium); 07 is TEST-UNIT-09. Refuted: 03, because the HTML value sanitization of datetime-local turns an impossible day like 2026-02-30 into '', so riyadhLocalToIsoOrNull never sees one (V8 does roll it to 2026-03-02); 04, because isHostedSite is exercised indirectly by turnstile.test.ts:111/125, email.test.ts:191/235-244 and moyasar-client.test.ts:71/211, and its only uncovered branch (an unparseable SITE_URL failing open for a test secret) cannot be reached, since every Turnstile caller first requires a parseable siteOrigin(); 08, already ruled on (AUDIT-2 X-HEALTH-1 refuted; the source is a private asset).

NOT DONE: a real mutation run (running tests was not allowed); the moyasar-emulator.test.ts body line by line; the integration suites beyond the guard questions (TEST-DB slice). With more time: run Stryker or a manual mutation pass on staff.ts, staff-admin, rate-limit, turnstile and backup-format; check every handler test's scripted SQL reply against the real SQL reply shapes (only checkout_cancel PAYMENT_ACTIVE was checked, and it matches); confirm the en-CA output on iOS 16 Safari.

Files: scratch C:\Users\alazi\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-alazi-Downloads-Tech-Code-My-projects-not-shipped-yet-ANASAQ-ME\e4be5ed4-f198-443e-9131-354d6dec169a\scratchpad\audit\TEST-UNIT\ holds findings.jsonl, prior-fix-checks.jsonl, plan-gaps.jsonl, doc-claims.jsonl, coverage.md, final.json, tz-demo.cjs and time-pdf.mjs.

## TEST-E2E
Read in full: playwright.config.ts; .github/workflows/ci.yml; package.json; tsconfig.json; vitest.config.ts; all 19 files of tests/e2e (auth, cart-checkout, checkout-api, cms, helpers, media, motion, no-js, order-page, orders-admin, orders-money, orders, owner-operations, product-availability, public, reader, shots, store-admin, visual); docs/development.md; PLANS/VERIFICATION.md; PLANS/ISSUES.md open items; HANDOFF.md:32; tests/integration/support.ts (harness setup and stop); tests/support/moyasar-emulator.ts (config, reset, redirects); the migrations behind the screens the specs assert on (staff_directory, order_access, order_summary, payment_view, outbox_claim, contact_submit, orders_alerts, refund_result); scripts/check-export.mjs, check-budgets.mjs, local-env.mjs; P08 round-12a record; AUDIT-1 TESTS.json; AUDIT-2 findings for e2e/CI; DESIGN-B commands.txt.

What each spec drives: auth (code sign-in, TOTP enrolment, invite through step-up; no denial cases, refuted in AUDIT-2 GAP-G1-12); public (one h1 and no console errors on 10 routes, menu, skip link, films, scenes filter and lightbox, contact validation, JS-off as DOM counts); no-js (header inline at 375, scenes links, journal, contact noscript, P08 pages offer no dead control; all under reduced motion); motion (counts Element.animate calls per element; proves reveals are scheduled and played, not that content ends visible); visual (13 routes x 4 widths, overflow <= 1px end side only, captures never compared; JS-off h1 only); cart-checkout/checkout-api (real quote/create/pay/cancel against the emulator; refusal and verify states partly mocked); orders (10 journeys x 2 widths on the real stack and emulator, no page.route: verified); order-page (order and notify pages, functions fully mocked; mock shapes match order_access/order_summary/payment_view and download refusals); product-availability (catalog_availability and notify mocked, quote patched, one real read); orders-admin/orders-money (real sign-in and harness-made orders; orders-money mocks every admin function reply, reconciliation lists mocked); store-admin (P07 admin CRUD, the switch with step-up, 11c preorder/paid file/sign-ups/stats against real functions with targeted mocks); owner-operations (contact/outbox/webhook/sweep at HTTP level, home, email problems, settings); cms, media, reader (real stack; reader PDF fixtures routed).

Mock policy: page.route fakes function or Data API answers in order-page, product-availability, orders-money, orders-admin, store-admin, cart-checkout and reader (inventory in my grep); orders.spec, checkout-api, cms, media, owner-operations, auth, public, no-js, motion and visual use none. Spot-checked mock fidelity (orders get, download, payments verify, admin STEP_UP_REQUIRED): faithful.

Widths: visual 360/768/1024/1440 (heights 1000, not the plan's 800/1024/768/900); P08 specs 360 and 1440; no-js 375; public menu 375/1280; reader 1440/390/360/900; media and admin screenshots 360/1440; none at 320, no zoom. Overflow of story pages is measured only under reduced motion.

CI: a green run proves lint errors (a11y rules are warnings), types (including the specs), unit tests, frozen sources, copy rules, Deno types, that every migration applies to a fresh Postgres, the content import, a static export with the 59 required files and no secret shape, RTL attributes and the JS budget of the pages that exist without products or posts. It proves nothing about RLS, grants, money SQL, functions at runtime, mail, payments or any browser journey; test:db and e2e never run there (confirmed) and cannot run against a hosted deployment. No automated link between a tested commit and the hosted site exists (plan gap).

Emulator's part: the journeys prove the code against the emulator's reading of Moyasar's docs, including faults; its stand-in page redirects to success_url verbatim, so orders.spec.ts:294's `\?order=N$` bakes in a choice Moyasar's docs leave open (P08 docs record), while the return page's tolerance of appended params is proven separately (cart-checkout.spec.ts:892-917, 1057-1065). Real invoice pages, webhook headers/retries and live behaviour stay under E02.

Mailpit retrieval: newest-first is correct; the stale-code race is TEST-E2E-05. 1,000 staff rows: a product truncation in staff_directory (TEST-E2E-03), plus pile-up from helpers that never retire users.

Measurements (scratch audit/TEST-E2E/measure): overflow-clip.cjs, visible-hidden.cjs, pdf-text.mjs. Nothing in the repo was run or changed; no request went to ports 54320-54329.

Lead disposition: lead TEST-E2E-01 (team screen truncation) confirmed as TEST-E2E-03 (its line 76 is 71). The previous attempt had no other leads.

Not reached or left for more time: host.docker.internal mapping by Supabase CLI 2.106.0 on Linux runners (needed for the full CI step); per-mock shape fidelity for the reconciliation_list, disputes_list and admin stats mocks; whether GoTrue rejects a reused TOTP code within its step (orders.spec answers several step-ups quickly); a timing run of test:db for the CI estimate.

## FIX-A1

Every one of AUDIT-1's 253 confirmed findings was checked against today's tree (base 3a30a91 plus the working tree), not a sample: both high, all 62 medium, all 172 low and all 17 info, including the 12 owner/recorded items (re-rated for checkout and payments). Each is a finding, plus FIX-A1-06 (S04.1's costs note stale since P08's three tiers) and FIX-A1-09 (X1.1's reset now closes a live store silently). G4.2 was reversed by owner decision D44 (consent), not by regression; S06.4, recorded open by AUDIT-1, is fixed by P08. Note: one record in prior_fix_checks with prior_id 'S22.17b-guard' is a stray placeholder and should be ignored. Read in full: AUDIT-1 REPORT.md, APPENDIX-findings.md and findings/<ROUND>.json (12 rounds, via a digest in SCRATCH/FIX-A1/a1-digest.txt); 20260930120000_audit_fixes.sql; the later definition of every function it touched (order_emails outbox_claim/replay/attention; audit2 outbox_result, publish_due, schedule_version, content_go_live, commerce_policies_approve, archive_document; payment_core catalog_audit and owner_alert; checkout_payment checkout_quote and checkout_create; stats_disputes buyer_retention_purge); tests/integration/audit-fixes.test.ts (structure, the S01.4/S01.5 bodies, and the diff since f38f04d). What a green CI proves: an exact lockfile install; ESLint over app, tests, scripts and functions; tsc over all .ts/.tsx (not the .mjs scripts); 68 unit files of pure helpers; frozen deploy/ hashes; copy rules; deno check of every function entry; every migration and the seed applying from zero; the content import; the static export with 59 required files, RTL attributes, no prefix-recognisable secret and no demo text against a non-loopback origin; the public JS budget. No vacuous step was found: the deno loop fails on an empty glob or a missing deno.json under bash -eo pipefail. None of VERIFICATION.md's non-negotiable invariant tests run in CI, nor any Edge Function over HTTP, e2e, axe or restore. Branch protection on main cannot be seen from here (no credentialed GitHub call made).

Lead: lead:SUPPLY-01 confirmed and extended as SUPPLY-03. Its severity is lowered to low because nothing reaches the vulnerable code, and its line is corrected from 36 to 40. The missing CI audit step became a plan gap. No lead was refuted.

Checked and not reported:
- The Deno side floats only iceberg-js ^0.8.1 (Supabase-owned, and 0.8.1 is the only 0.8.x release), because supabase-js 2.117.1 pins its sub-packages. So `--no-lock` changes nothing today, but CI and a deploy could drift apart on a future 0.8.x.
- The backup format is sound: AES-256-GCM with the header as AAD, scrypt N=2^17 r=8 p=1, and a fresh salt and IV per file. Truncation, reordering, a swapped entry or an edited header all fail the tag. Plaintext is written before the tag is checked, but it is deleted on failure; only a killed process leaves it behind.
- The env passphrase is inherited by every supabase child process (exposed to the same user only).
- The 2.106.0 data.sql also carries pg_net's queue and response tables. A captured queue row would include the jobs bearer secret. Current pg_net sends or purges only after a wake, so a rehearsal does not replay it.
- check:export skips .mjs and .svg (one pdf worker file).
- npx-run tools are version-pinned, but their transitive trees float and npm runs their install scripts; deploy.cjs does this with the Cloudflare token in its environment.
- Next telemetry is on during builds.
- GHSA-vq4v and GHSA-5wx6 need tarball or URL dependencies, and the lockfile has none today.
- The `..` folder edge case also applies to backup --out (an encrypted file).
- Per the owner's standing order, font licences were not examined.

Not reached / next with more time:
- Run the SUPPLY-05 and SUPPLY-11 proofs on a disposable machine and network.
- Check whether hosted GoTrue encrypts auth.mfa_factors secrets with a project key (a restored owner's TOTP might not verify on a new project).
- Compare the Edge Runtime's Deno version with CI's 2.9.6.
- Check the Windows Firewall rules for Docker Desktop on the owner's machine.
- Check GitHub branch protection.
